\displaystyle g_{G}^{-2}(\frac{1}{4}\tilde{\eta}^{cd\mspace{1mu}c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\phi^{2}F_{cd}^{a}F_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}-6\eta^{cd}\eth_{c}\phi\eth_{d}\phi)\},

where we have introduced the following definition:

\displaystyle\Psi_{-}\to\varPsi_{-}\equiv\phi^{3/2}\Psi_{-},

(86)

and made the replacement in the second formalism of the action:

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{a}^{bc}\to g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{a}^{bc},\quad A_{a}\to g_{E}A_{a},

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{a}\equiv i\eth_{a}+{\mathcal{A}}_{a}+A_{a}\to i{\mathcal{D}}_{a}\equiv i\eth_{a}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{a}^{bc}\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{bc}+g_{E}A_{a},

(87)

Such a redefined chirality-based Dirac spinor
\varPsi_{-}
has a local scaling charge
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=3/2
and canonical dimension
\mathcal{C}_{d}=3/2
. Note that the covariant derivative
{\mathcal{D}}_{c}
and the integral measure
[\delta^{4}\zeta]
have local scaling charges
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=1
and
\check{\mathcal{C}}_{s}=-4
, respectively.

When making special scaling gauge transformation and taking the scaling gauge fixing condition to be Einstein basis, i.e.,
\phi\to M_{\kappa}
and
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}\to\chi_{\mu}^{(e)a}
as shown in Eqs.(
77
)-(
VI.1
), we are able to express the gauge invariant action in Eq.(
85
) into the following simple form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{4}\zeta^{(e)}]\,{\mathcal{L}}^{(e)}

(88)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[\delta^{4}\zeta^{(e)}]\mspace{1mu}\{(\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{a}\varPsi_{-}-m\mspace{1mu}\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{6}\varPsi_{-}+H.c.)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\eta^{cc^{\prime}}\eta^{dd^{\prime}}F_{cd}F_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta^{cc^{\prime}}\eta^{dd^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{cd}^{\;ab}{\mathcal{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}ab}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\bar{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}m_{G}^{2}{\mathbf{F}}_{cd}^{a}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}+g_{G}^{-2}M_{\kappa}^{2}\frac{1}{4}\tilde{\eta}^{cd\mspace{1mu}c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}F_{cd}^{a}F_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}\}.

VI.3
Gauge and scaling invariant action with gauge-gravity-geometry correspondence in gravitational quantum field theory

As the gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
(
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\,\mu}
) or
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\,\mu}
) plays a role as Goldstone-like boson and appears as a bi-covariant vector field in biframe spacetime, it enables us to project the above actions built in locally flat gravigauge spacetime into the ones represented in biframe spacetime within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory.

In light of the scaling gauge invariant fields, we can rewrite the spin gauge invariant action into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\mathfrak{A}(x)\,\mathfrak{L}

(89)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\mathfrak{A}(x)\mspace{1mu}\{(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\nu}\bar{\Psi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{a}\mathbf{A}_{\mu a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\Psi_{-}-\frac{m}{M_{\kappa}}\bar{\Psi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{6}\Psi_{-}+H.c.)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;ab}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}ab}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;a}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{4}g_{G}^{-2}\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\mathsf{F}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}\},

where we have used the definitions:

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}\to g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab},\quad A_{\mu}\to g_{E}A_{\mu},

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\equiv i\partial_{\nu}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}+A_{\nu}\equiv i\partial_{\nu}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}^{bc}\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{bc}+g_{E}A_{\nu},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\tilde{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}.

(90)

In terms of the local scaling charged fields, we arrive at the following spin gauge and scaling gauge invariant action:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\,{\mathcal{L}}

(91)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\mspace{1mu}\{(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{a}\chi_{\mu a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\varPsi_{-}-\frac{m}{M_{\kappa}}\phi\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{6}\varPsi_{-}+H.c.)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;ab}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}ab}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}\phi^{2}\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}({\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;a}+S_{\mu\nu}^{\;a})({\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}+S_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle g_{G}^{-2}(\frac{1}{4}\phi^{2}\tilde{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}-6\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\phi\partial_{\nu}\phi\},

with the definitions:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\equiv i\partial_{\nu}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}+A_{\nu}\equiv i\partial_{\nu}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu}^{bc}\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{bc}+g_{E}A_{\nu},

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\chi}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\chi}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}=\phi^{4}\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\chi}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\chi}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\tilde{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}=\phi^{4}\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\chi(x)\equiv\det\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)=\phi^{-4}\mathfrak{A}(x),\quad\hat{\chi}\equiv\det\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}.

(92)

When making the scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein basis, we are able to simplify the above action into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\mspace{1mu}{\mathcal{L}}^{(e)}

(93)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\mspace{1mu}\{(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{a}\chi_{\mu a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\varPsi_{-}-m\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\Gamma^{6}\varPsi_{-}+H.c.)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;ab}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}ab}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}m_{G}^{2}\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;a}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{4}g_{G}^{-2}M_{\kappa}^{2}\tilde{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}\},

where all scaling charged fields are understood to be in Einstein basis and the index ‘(
e
)’ appearing in the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{(e)a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{(e)\mu}
) have been ignored for convenience.

It can be verified from the relation in Eq.(
VI.2
) that the gravitational gauge interaction characterized by the gravigauge field is related to Einstein-Hilbert action up to a total derivative as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\chi\,\tilde{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}=\chi\,R+2\partial_{\mu}(\chi\hat{\chi}^{\mu\rho}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\sigma}F_{\sigma\rho}^{a}),

(94)

where we have used the identities:

\displaystyle R\equiv\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\nu}R_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\equiv\eta^{cb}R_{cb}\equiv\eta_{b}^{\;c}\eta_{a}^{\;d}R_{cd}^{\;ab}\equiv\hat{\chi}^{\mu\sigma}R_{\mu\sigma}\equiv\hat{\chi}^{\mu\sigma}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\rho}R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}.

(95)

Such relation and identities reveal the gauge-gravity-geometry correspondence.

Geometrically,
R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
is the so-called Riemann curvature tensor,
R_{\mu\sigma}
and
R
correspond to the Ricci curvature tensor and Ricci curvature scalar.
R_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}
is explicitly given by,

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\;\rho}(x)=\partial_{\mu}\Gamma_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\partial_{\nu}\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}+\Gamma_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}\Gamma_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}-\Gamma_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda},

(96)

with
\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}(x)
defined as follows:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\sigma}^{\;\;a}+\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\chi_{\sigma}^{\;\;b},

(97)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\lambda}(\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\lambda\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}\chi_{\lambda\mu}-\partial_{\lambda}\chi_{\mu\sigma})=\Gamma_{\sigma\mu}^{\rho},

which is the so-called affine connection or Christoffel symbol in geometry.

When applying for the gauge-gravity-geometry correspondence and adopting the vector-like spinor representation of Dirac fermion in four-dimensional Hilbert space, the action in Eq.(
93
) can be further simplified into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\mspace{1mu}{\mathcal{L}}^{(e)}

(98)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\;\{\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\bar{\psi}\gamma^{a}\chi_{\mu a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\psi+H.c.)-m\bar{\psi}\psi

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;ab}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}ab}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}m_{G}^{2}\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;a}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}}\,R\,\}-2\partial_{\mu}(\chi\hat{\chi}^{\mu\rho}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\sigma}F_{\rho\sigma}^{a}),

with
G_{N}
the gravitational constant,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}}\equiv\frac{M_{\kappa}^{2}}{g_{G}^{2}}\equiv\frac{M_{P}^{2}}{16\pi}.

(99)

So far, we have provided various formalisms for the gauge and scaling invariant action of Dirac spinor field within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory following along the action principle of path integral formulation, which indicate the gauge-gravity and gravity-geometry correspondences and lead to the gauge-geometry duality relation.

VI.4
Hidden gauge formalism of the action with flowing unitary gauge and the gauge-geometry duality with emergent general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R)

The above formalisms of the action are built based on the scaling and gauge invariance principle through the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. The coordinate spacetime remains to maintain the Poincaré group symmetry in globally flat Minkowski spacetime. The gauge fixing
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field as a bicovariant vector field spanned in biframe spacetime is shown to play an essential role as a projection operator. In general, such a projection operator should allow us to define a spacetime gauge field from the spin gauge field and provide an equivalent action in hidden gauge formalism, so that we are able to investigate more profound correlation between the gravitational interaction and Riemann geometry of spacetime.

Let us demonstrate explicitly how the general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R) emerges automatically as a consequence of gauge invariance principle, which corroborates the gauge-geometry duality in curved Riemannian spacetime. The
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field with imposing the gauge fixing condition turns out to be characterized by the gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
, which is associated in general with the spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}
through the following covariant relation of spin gauge symmetry:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;b}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\mathbf{A}_{\rho}^{\;\;a}=0.

(100)

Such a relation enables us to define a hidden spin gauge invariant field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
from the spin gauge field as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;b}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;a},

(101)

which brings on a gauge field represented in coordinate spacetime. For short, we may refer to such a gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
as a
spacetime gauge field
.

As shown in Eq.(
31
), the spin gauge symmetry gets a gravitational origin by decomposing the spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}
into the sum of spin gravigauge field
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mu}^{ab}
and covariant-gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}
. In light of such a decomposition, the spacetime gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
can be devided into the corresponding two parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}+\mathbf{A}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho},

(102)

with
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
satisfying the following gauge covariant relation:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;b}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\mathbf{A}_{\rho}^{\;\;a}=0,

(103)

and
\mathbf{A}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
being defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\mathbf{A}_{\mu b}^{a}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;b}.

(104)

From the relation Eq.(
103
) and the explicit form of
\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}
presented in Eq.(
III.2
), we arrive at the following form for
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;\;b},

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\rho\sigma}(\partial_{\mu}\mathsf{H}_{\sigma\nu}+\partial_{\nu}\mathsf{H}_{\sigma\mu}-\partial_{\sigma}\mathsf{H}_{\mu\nu})=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\nu\mu}^{\rho},

which comes to the identity given in Eq.(
97
).

The gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
may be regarded as
Goldstone-like boson field
which transforms as bicovariant vector field under both spin gauge group and Lorentz group transformations. In light of the spacetime gauge field, the spin gauge symmetry SP(1,3) is transmuted into a hidden gauge symmetry. The symmetric tensor fields
\mathsf{H}_{\mu\nu}
and
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\nu}
as the product of two gravigauge fields by contracting the vector indices in locally flat gravigauge spacetime are considered as composite fields with both hidden spin gauge symmetry and scaling gauge symmetry, which are referred to as Goldstone-like dual
gravimetric fields
geometrically.

The symmetric gauge field
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}=\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\nu\mu}^{\rho}
is completely determined by the dual Goldstone-like gravimetric fields
\mathsf{H}_{\mu\nu}
and
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\nu}
, we may refer to
\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
as scaling gauge invariant
spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
. For convenience of mention, the gauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
is called as
spacetime covariant-gauge field
. In terms of the spacetime gauge field, we obtain the corresponding field strength:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}=\nabla_{\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\nabla_{\nu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\nu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}=\partial_{\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda},

(105)

which can be rewritten into the following two parts in correspondence to the decomposition of spacetime gauge field shown in Eq.(
102
):

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}\equiv\mathbf{R}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}+{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho},

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\;\rho}=\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\partial_{\nu}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}=\nabla_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\nabla_{\nu}\mathbf{A}_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}+\mathbf{A}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}\mathbf{A}_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}-\mathbf{A}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}\mathbf{A}_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}=\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda}\mathbf{A}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}\mathbf{A}_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}.

(106)

Such defined tensor fields
\mathbf{R}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\;\rho}
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\;\rho}
in the hidden gauge formalism are referred to as scaling gauge invariant
spacetime gravimetric-gauge field strength
and
spacetime covariant-gauge field strength
, respectively.

In terms of the dimensionless gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
), we arrive at the following gauge covariant relation:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;b}-\mathsf{A}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\chi_{\rho}^{\;\;a}=0.

(107)

Again decomposing the spin gauge field into two parts:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\equiv\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}+\mathsf{A}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a},

with the spin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}
being the so-called
spin connection
, which is completely determined by the dimensionless gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
) as presented in Eqs.(
58
) and (
V.1
). In this case, the spacetime gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
can also be expressed into the following form:

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}+A_{\mu\nu}^{\rho},

(108)

where the spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
satisfies the well-known identity:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;b}-\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\chi_{\rho}^{\;\;a}=0,

(109)

with the explicit form:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}(x)=\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\lambda}(\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\lambda\nu}+\partial_{\nu}\chi_{\lambda\mu}-\partial_{\lambda}\chi_{\mu\nu})=\Gamma_{\nu\mu}^{\rho},

which is called as
affine connection
or
Christoffel symbol
represented in Eq.(
97
). The spacetime covariant-gauge field
A_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle A_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\mathsf{A}_{\mu b}^{a}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;b}.

(110)

It is noticed that by directly taking the symmetric feature of
\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
, i.e.,
\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}=\Gamma_{\nu\mu}^{\rho}
, one can obtain from the relation in Eq.(
109
) the explicit form of spin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}
in Eq.(
V.1
) by simply solving the following equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}-\partial_{\nu}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;b}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{\nu\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;b}=0.

(111)

The corresponding spacetime gauge field strength can be written into the following form:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}\equiv R_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}+F_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho},

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\;\rho}=\partial_{\mu}\Gamma_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\partial_{\nu}\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}+\Gamma_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}\Gamma_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}-\Gamma_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda},

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}=\nabla_{\mu}A_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\nabla_{\nu}A_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho}+A_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}A_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}-A_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}A_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}A_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu\sigma}^{\rho}-\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\lambda}A_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}+\Gamma_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}A_{\nu\sigma}^{\lambda}.

(112)

where
R_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}
is called as Riemann curvature tensor presented in Eq.(
96
). In general, we can verify the following relations:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho\sigma}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu\sigma^{\prime}}^{\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\rho^{\prime}\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\sigma}=-{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\sigma\rho},

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}^{\rho\sigma}=R_{\mu\nu\sigma^{\prime}}^{\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}=R_{\mu\nu\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\rho^{\prime}\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}=R_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\sigma}=-R_{\mu\nu}^{\sigma\rho},

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}^{\rho\sigma}=F_{\mu\nu\sigma^{\prime}}^{\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}=F_{\mu\nu\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\rho^{\prime}\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma^{\prime}\sigma}=F_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\sigma}=-F_{\mu\nu}^{\sigma\rho},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}={\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho\sigma}\chi_{\rho}^{\;a}\chi_{\sigma}^{\;b}=\hat{\chi}^{a\rho}\hat{\chi}^{b\sigma}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}

(113)

by using the following general identities of covariant derivative:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}=\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu b}^{a}\chi_{\nu}^{\;b}-\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\chi_{\rho}^{\;a}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\rho}=\partial_{\mu}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\nu}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu b}^{a}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}+\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\nu}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\chi_{\rho\sigma}=\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\rho\sigma}-\Gamma_{\mu\rho}^{\nu}\chi_{\nu\sigma}-\Gamma_{\mu\sigma}^{\nu}\chi_{\rho\nu}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\sigma}=\partial_{\mu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\sigma}+\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\sigma}+\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\sigma}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\nu}=0,

(114)

By taking the above identities and relations, we can reformulate the actions in Eqs.(
80
) and (
89
) into the following alternative form in hidden gauge formalism:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\mathfrak{A}(x)\,\mathfrak{L}

(115)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\mathfrak{A}(x)\mspace{1mu}\{(\bar{\Psi}_{-}\hat{\Sigma}_{-}^{\mu}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\Psi_{-}-\frac{m}{M_{\kappa}}\bar{\Psi}_{-}\Sigma_{-}^{6}\Psi_{-}+H.c.)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;\rho\sigma}\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}\rho\sigma}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;\rho}\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{4}g_{G}^{-2}\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\boldsymbol{\mathsf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}\},

where we have used the following definitions in the hidden gauge formalism:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{F}}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;\rho}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\rho}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;a},\quad\boldsymbol{\mathsf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;\rho}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\rho}\mathsf{F}_{\mu\nu}^{\;a},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{\rho^{\prime}\rho}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\rho}^{\;a}\mathbf{A}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\;a^{\prime}}\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}},\quad\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\rho}^{\;a}\mathbf{A}_{\rho^{\prime}}^{\;a^{\prime}}\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}},

(116)

with
\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}
and
\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}
defined in Eq.(
VI.3
), and

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\equiv i\partial_{\mu}+(\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]})\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\rho\sigma},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\rho\sigma}\equiv\mathsf{H}_{\rho\rho^{\prime}}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\sigma}^{\rho^{\prime}},\quad\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mu\rho\sigma}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\rho}^{\;\;a}\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mu ab}\mathbf{A}_{\sigma}^{\;\;b},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\rho\sigma}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\rho\sigma\mu}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\sigma\mu\rho}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\mathbf{\Omega}_{\mu\rho\sigma}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\rho\sigma\mu}+\mathbf{\Omega}_{\sigma\mu\rho})

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mu}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mu}\varGamma_{-},\quad\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mu}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\varGamma^{a},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\frac{i}{4}[\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mu},\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\nu}]=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}\Sigma^{ab}.

(117)

It is noted that the
spacetime gravigauge field

\mathbf{\Omega}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}
actually reflects the gravitational origin of gauge symmetry. The
\Gamma
-matrices
\hat{\mathbf{\Gamma}}^{\mu}
are regarded as
gravigauge-dressed
\Gamma
-matrices
as they are no longer constant matrices. The matrices
\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}^{\mu\nu}
and
\hat{\mathbf{\Sigma}}_{-}^{\mu}
provide the
gravigauge-dressed inhomogeneous spin group generators
.

By choosing the scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein basis, the above action can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}_{D}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\mspace{1mu}{\mathcal{L}}^{(e)}

(118)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}x]\chi(x)\;\{\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\hat{\gamma}^{\mu}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\psi+H.c.)-m\bar{\psi}\psi

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\;\rho\sigma}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}\rho\sigma}+\frac{1}{16\pi G_{N}}\,R

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}(\alpha_{G}-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{W})m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}(\mathsf{A}_{\mu\rho\sigma}-\Gamma_{\mu\rho\sigma}/g_{G})(\mathsf{A}_{\mu^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}-\Gamma_{\mu^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}/g_{G})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}(\alpha_{G}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{W})m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\mathsf{A}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}\mathsf{A}_{[\mu^{\prime}\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]},

with the definitions:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\equiv i\partial_{\mu}+(\mathsf{\Omega}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}+g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]})\frac{1}{2}\hat{\Sigma}^{\rho\sigma},

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\mathsf{A}_{\mu\rho\sigma}+\mathsf{A}_{\rho\sigma\mu}+\mathsf{A}_{\sigma\mu\rho}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Omega}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu\rho\sigma}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\rho\sigma\mu}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{\sigma\mu\rho})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\;\;\;\equiv\frac{1}{3}(\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\rho}^{\;\;c}\,\chi_{\sigma c}+\partial_{\rho}\chi_{\sigma}^{\;\;c}\,\chi_{\mu c}+\partial_{\sigma}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;c}\,\chi_{\rho c}),

\displaystyle\hat{\Sigma}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\frac{i}{4}[\hat{\gamma}^{\mu},\hat{\gamma}^{\nu}]=\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}\Sigma^{ab},\quad\hat{\gamma}^{\mu}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}\gamma^{a}.

(119)

It becomes manifest that the actions presented in Eqs.(
115
) and (
118
) bring on an emergent general linear group symmetry in coordinate spacetime:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{GL(}1,3,\mbox{R)},

(120)

which lays the foundation for Einstein theory of GR and governs the gravitational interaction in curved Riemannian spacetime. It indicates that such a group symmetry actually appears as a hidden local symmetry in the action built based on the scaling and gauge invariance principle. In general, the action in Eq.(
115
) reformulated in the hidden gauge formalism possesses the following maximal joint symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{GL(}1,3,\mbox{R)}\Join\mbox{WS(}1,3),

(121)

which extends naturally the global Poincaré group symmetry PO(1,3) in globally flat Minkowski spacetime to be a local group symmetry GL(1,3,R) in curved Riemannian spacetime with emergence of Riemann geometry.

It is noted that the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry WS(1,3) still holds for the chirality-based Dirac spinor field in the action Eq.(
115
) though it appears as a hidden spin gauge symmetry. This can be seen from the coupling of totally antisymmetric spacetime gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{[\mu\rho\sigma]}
which is characterized by the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
) rather than gravimetric field
\chi_{\mu\nu}
(
\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}
). It is interesting to notice that the spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\Gamma_{\mu\rho}^{\sigma}
actually decouples from the Dirac spinor field due to its symmetric property
\Gamma_{\mu\rho}^{\sigma}=\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\sigma}
and the hermiticity requirement of the action. Nevertheless, in the purely bosonic interactions of the action, the spacetime gauge interaction is described by the antisymmetric spacetime gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{\rho\sigma}=-\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{\sigma\rho}
together with the symmetric gravimetric field
\chi_{\mu\nu}
which characterizes the symmetric spacetime gravimetric-gauge field
\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}=\Gamma_{\nu\mu}^{\rho}
as affine connection/Christoffel symbols.

It is observed that the symmetric Goldstone-like gravimetric field
\chi_{\mu\nu}
concerns
10
degrees of freedom, while the basic gravitational field characterized by the Goldstone-like gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
contains
16
degrees of freedom, which involves additional six degrees of freedom. Such an extra degrees of freedom reflect exactly the equivalence classes of spin gauge symmetry SP(1,3), which corresponds to the six group parameters of SP(1,3) gauge transformation. Such an observation motivates us to eliminate the redundant degrees of freedom caused from the spin gauge symmetry by simply making a gauge prescription of spin gauge symmetry SP(1,3).

Unlike the usual internal gauge symmetry, the spin gauge symmetry gets a gravitational origin characterized by the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu a}(x)
, a simple gauge prescription should be realized by taking an appropriate spin gauge transformation. Let us choose such a spin gauge transformation
\tilde{\Lambda}_{\;\,a}^{b}(x)
that transmutes the Goldstone-like gravigauge field into the following symmetric one:

\displaystyle\chi_{\mu a}(x)\to\tilde{\chi}_{\mu a}(x)=\chi_{\mu b}(x)\tilde{\Lambda}^{b}_{\;\,a}(x)=\tilde{\chi}_{a\mu}(x),

(122)

which presents a natural gauge fixing condition of spin gauge symmetry SP(1,3). We may refer to such a gauge prescription as
flowing unitary gauge
, which holds locally for a coordinate system at point to point in spacetime. In such a flowing unitary gauge, the Goldstone-like symmetric gravigauge field
\tilde{\chi}_{\mu a}(x)
gets exactly the same degrees of freedom as the gravimetric field
\chi_{\mu\nu}(x)
via the hidden gauge symmetry, i.e.:

\displaystyle\chi_{\mu\nu}(x)=\chi_{\mu a}(x)\eta^{ab}\chi_{\nu b}(x)\equiv\tilde{\chi}_{\mu a}(x)\eta^{ab}\tilde{\chi}_{\nu b}(x)\equiv(\tilde{\chi}_{\mu a}(x))^{2}.

(123)

When fixing gauge to be in the flowing unitary gauge, the independent degrees of freedom for the spacetime gauge interactions are represented by the symmetric Goldstone-like gravigauge field
\tilde{\chi}_{\mu a}(x)=\tilde{\chi}_{a\mu}(x)
(or symmetric Goldstone-like gravimetric field
\chi_{\mu\nu}(x)\equiv(\tilde{\chi}_{\mu a}(x))^{2}
) and the spacetime gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{\rho\sigma}(x)
. Meanwhile, the total independent degrees of freedom in the theory should remain unchanged since the extra degrees of freedom appearing in the Goldstone-like gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu a}(x)
are actually absorbed into the antisymmetric spacetime gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{\rho\sigma}(x)
which behaves as a massive-like gauge field as shown in Eq.(
118
). It can be checked that in such a flowing unitary gauge, the action can still possess an associated global symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{SO}(1,3)\,\widetilde{\Join}\,\mbox{SP}(1,3),

(124)

which transforms the gauging fixing symmetric gravigauge field
\tilde{\chi}_{\mu a}(x)
to remain symmetric, i.e.,
\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{\mu a}(x^{\prime})=\tilde{\chi}^{\prime}_{a\mu}(x^{\prime})
.

We now come to the conclusion that
the laws of nature should be independent of the choice of coordinate systems
, which arises from the intriguing observation that when the fundamental symmetry of basic fields in Hilbert space is gauged to be local symmetry by following along the scaling and gauge invariance principle, the fundamental symmetry of coordinates in Minkowski spacetime automatically obeys the general covariance principle of coordinate system with the emergence of general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R) and the genesis of Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian spacetime, which indicates that the scaling and gauge invariance principle plays an essential role not only to the fundamental symmetry of basic fields in Hilbert space but also to the fundamental symmetry in coordinate spacetime. In fact, such a feature reflects intrinsically a
gauge-geometry duality
in GQFT.

VI.5
Finiteness and renormalizability of gravitational quantum field theory

Before studying the gravidynamics and spinodynamics as well as electrodynamics, it is useful to present a general analysis and discussion on the finiteness and renormalizability of GQFT though the investigation on the quantum contributions to the dynamics of basic fields is beyond the scope of present paper.
Actually, the finiteness and renormalizability have been long-standing issues involved in all QFTs established based on the special relativity and quantum mechanics. In the GQFT, there gets the universal coupling with the dual gravigauge field
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\mu}(x)
or dual gravimetric field
\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}(x)
as gravitational interactions, which is usually thought to bring on the nonlinear nature of the theory and result in nonrenormalizable high dimensionful interaction terms which leads to the non-renormalizability of the theory in the usual sense of renormalizability, it means that the divergencies of quantum loop effects cannot be absorbed into the basic fields and coupling constants of the theory.

To get better understanding on the finiteness and renormalizability of GQFT, let us first briefly look through the issues on the finiteness and renormalizability of QFT. QFT has provided a very successful description on the microscopic world, especially it has been well tested in applying to the SM which was built to describe the three basic forces of nature governed by the gauge symmetries
SU(3)_{c}\times SU(2)_{L}\times U(1)_{Y}
. Nevertheless, in the perturbative treatment of QFT, it still meets the ultraviolet (UV) divergence problem due to infinite Feynman integrals involved in the closed loops of virtual particles, which may destroy the basic symmetries of original theory. Mathematically, the divergence is caused from the integral region where all virtual particles will get infinite large energy momentum. Physically, it arises from the very short wavelength/high frequency fluctuations of basic fields in the path integral. To deal with the divergences, the so-called renormalization scheme is developed to absorb all divergences into basic fields and coupling constants in the theory through their redefinitions, which is referred to as the renormalizability of the theory.

The theoretical consistency of the SM mainly relies on its renormalizability within the framework of QFT. Nevertheless, such a renormalization scheme does not satisfy to all physicists, like Dirac and Feynman, which may be seen from their criticisms
PDirac
;
HKragh
;
RFeynman
.

Dirac has commented that “Most physicists are very satisfied with the situation. They say: ‘Quantum electrodynamics (QED) is a good theory and we do not have to worry about it any more.’ I must say that I am very dissatisfied with the situation, because this so-called ‘good theory’ does involve neglecting infinities which appear in its equations, neglecting them in an arbitrary way. This is just not sensible mathematics. Sensible mathematics involves neglecting a quantity when it is small, not neglecting it just because it is infinitely great and you do not want it.”

Feynman has also remarked that “The shell game that we play
\dots
is technically called ‘renormalization’. But no matter how clever the word, it is still what I would call a dippy process! Having to resort to such hocus-pocus has prevented us from proving that the theory of quantum electrodynamics (QED) is mathematically self-consistent. It’s surprising that the theory still hasn’t been proved self-consistent one way or the other by now; I suspect that renormalization is not mathematically legitimate.”

Therefore, QFT becomes well-defined only when it can be regularized properly to avoid the infinities through appropriate regularization method. Namely, such a renormalization scheme should eliminate divergences and get only finite quantities. On the other hand, the development of renormalization group technique has inspired us to figure out a well-defined QFT with the presence of physically meaningful energy scale. Nevertheless, the usual regularization schemes always bear some limitations for realizing a satisfied description on QFT. To achieve an infinity-free and symmetry-preserving regularization scheme, we have developed a novel regularization method which is the so-called loop regularization (LORE) method
LORE1
;
LORE2
. Such a LORE method has turned out to be a consistent regularization scheme which enables us to introduce intrinsically the physically meaningful energy scale and avoid infinities without spoiling basic symmetries of original theory. For a detailed description on the LORE method, it is referred to the review article
LORE3
.

The key concept in LORE method is the so-called irreducible loop integrals (ILIs) which are evaluated from the Feynman integrals by using Feynman parametrization and ultraviolet divergence-preserving (UVDP) parametrization
LORE1
;
LORE2
. For illustration on the ILIs, let us represent the one loop calculation of Feynman diagrams, where all Feynman integrals of the one-particle irreducible graphs can be expressed into the following sets of loop integrals by adopting the Feynman parametrization method:

\displaystyle I_{-2\alpha}=\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}\ \frac{1}{(k^{2}-{\cal M}^{2})^{2+\alpha}},

\displaystyle I_{-2\alpha\ \mu\nu}=\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}\ \frac{k_{\mu}k_{\nu}}{(k^{2}-{\cal M}^{2})^{3+\alpha}},

\displaystyle I_{-2\alpha\ \mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}\ \frac{k_{\mu}k_{\nu}k_{\rho}k_{\sigma}}{(k^{2}-{\cal M}^{2})^{4+\alpha}},

(125)

with
\alpha=-1,0,1,\cdots
. Where the subscript (
-2\alpha
) labels the power counting dimension of energy momentum in the loop integrals. For the cases with
\alpha=-1
and
\alpha=0
, we arrive at the scalar and tensor type ILIs corresponding to the quadratic divergent integrals (
I_{2}
,
I_{2\mu\nu}\cdots
) and logarithmic divergent integrals (
I_{0}
,
I_{0\mu\nu}\cdots
).
{\cal M}^{2}
is the mass factor resulted as a function of Feynman parameters and external momenta
p_{i}
,
{\cal M}^{2}={\cal M}^{2}(m_{1}^{2},p_{1}^{2},\cdots)
. For the high loop overlapping Feynman integrals, the ILIs are evaluated to get rid of, in the denominator, the overlapping momentum factors
(k_{i}-k_{j}+p_{ij})^{2}

(i\neq j)
which appear in the original overlapping Feynman integrals of loop momenta
k_{i}
(
i=1,2,\cdots
), and eliminate the scalar momentum factors
k^{2}
in the numerator.

The essential feature demanded for a consistent regularization scheme is that it must preserve the symmetry principles of original theory, such as gauge invariance, Lorentz invariance and translational invariance, and meanwhile avoid the infinities of loop integrals with maintaining the initial divergent behavior and structure of original theory, such as quadratic and logarithmic divergent behaviors and structures. It can generally be proved that to preserve the gauge invariance principle in QFT, the regularized tensor-type and scalar-type ILIs should satisfy the following necessary and sufficient conditions:

\displaystyle I_{2\mu\nu}^{R}=\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\ I_{2}^{R},\quad I_{2\mu\nu\rho\sigma}^{R}=\frac{1}{8}(g_{\mu\nu}g_{\rho\sigma}+g_{\mu\rho}g_{\nu\sigma}+g_{\mu\sigma}g_{\rho\nu})\ I_{2}^{R},

\displaystyle I_{0\mu\nu}^{R}=\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu}\ I_{0}^{R},\quad I_{0\mu\nu\rho\sigma}^{R}=\frac{1}{24}(g_{\mu\nu}g_{\rho\sigma}+g_{\mu\rho}g_{\nu\sigma}+g_{\mu\sigma}g_{\rho\nu})\ I_{0}^{R},

(126)

which is referred to as the gauge invariance consistency conditions
LORE1
;
LORE2
;
LORE3
.

To achieve such consistency conditions, a simple regularization prescription in LORE method has consistently be realized as follows: firstly rotating the momentum to the Euclidean space via a Wick rotation and then replacing the loop integrating variable
k^{2}
and loop integrating measure
\int{d^{4}k}
of the ILIs through the corresponding regularized ones
[k^{2}]_{l}
and
\int[d^{4}k]_{l}
, i.e.:

\displaystyle k^{2}

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle[k^{2}]_{l}\equiv k^{2}+M^{2}_{l}\ ,

(127)

\displaystyle\int{d^{4}k}\ {\cal F}(k^{2})

\displaystyle\rightarrow

\displaystyle\int[d^{4}k]_{l}\ {\cal F}(k^{2}+M^{2}_{l})

(128)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int{d^{4}k}\lim_{N,M_{i}^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{N}c_{l}^{N}\ {\cal F}(k^{2}+M^{2}_{l})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{N,M_{i}^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{N}c_{l}^{N}\ \int{d^{4}k}\ {\cal F}(k^{2}+M^{2}_{l}).

Where
M_{l}^{2}
(
l=0,1,\ \cdots
) are regarded as regulator masses and
{\cal F}(k^{2})
is considered to be any integration function. The notation
\lim_{N,M_{i}^{2}}
denotes the limiting case
\lim_{N\to\infty,M_{i}^{2}\rightarrow\infty}
(
i=1,2,\cdots,N
).

The key point in LORE is to choose the appropriate coefficients
c_{l}^{N}
, so that any loop divergence with the power counting dimension larger than or equal to the space-time dimension will vanish. From that, we come to the general conditions for the coefficients
c_{l}^{N}
as follows:

\displaystyle\int d^{4}k\ \lim_{N,M_{i}^{2}}\sum_{l=0}^{N}c_{l}^{N}(k^{2}+M_{l}^{2})^{n}=0,\quad(n=0,1,\cdots),

(129)

which brings on the following relations for the regulator masses:

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{N}c_{l}^{N}(M_{l}^{2})^{n}=0,\quad(n=0,1,\cdots),

(130)

where we shall choose
M_{0}^{2}\equiv\mu_{s}^{2}\to 0
and
c_{0}^{N}=1
as the initial conditions in order to recover the original integrals in the limits
M_{i}^{2}\to\infty
(
i=1,2,\cdots,N
) and
N\to\infty
.

To completely fix the coefficients
c_{l}^{N}
and make them independent of the regulator masses, it is simple to take the following string-mode regulators:

M_{l}^{2}=\mu_{s}^{2}+lM_{R}^{2},\qquad l=0,1,2,\cdots,

(131)

which enables us to determine uniquely the coefficients
c_{l}^{N}
from the general relations of regulator masses given in Eq.(
130
) and obtain the following explicit solution:

c_{l}^{N}=(-1)^{l}\frac{N!}{(N-l)!l!}

(132)

which is known as the sign-associated Combinations. The factor
(-1)^{l}c_{l}^{N}
may be regarded as the number of combinations of
N
regulators taken
l
at a time.

To be more explicit and concrete, when applying such a regularization prescription in the LORE method with the simple solution of the regulators to the quadratically and logarithmically divergent ILIs, the divergent integrations become well-defined and can safely be performed to obtain the regularized divergent ILIs
I_{2}^{R}
and
I^{R}_{2\mu\nu}
as well as
I_{0}^{R}
and
I^{R}_{0\mu\nu}
LORE1
;
LORE2
;
LORE3
. We just represent them as follows:

\displaystyle I_{2}^{R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-i}{16\pi^{2}}\{M_{c}^{2}-\mu_{M}^{2}-\mu_{M}^{2}[\ln\frac{M_{c}^{2}}{\mu_{M}^{2}}-\gamma_{E}+\varepsilon(\frac{\mu_{M}^{2}}{M_{c}^{2}})+\varepsilon^{\prime}(\frac{\mu_{M}^{2}}{M_{c}^{2}})-1]\},

\displaystyle I_{0}^{R}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{16\pi^{2}}[\ln\frac{M_{c}^{2}}{\mu_{M}^{2}}-\gamma_{E}+\varepsilon(\frac{\mu_{M}^{2}}{M_{c}^{2}})],

\displaystyle I^{R}_{2\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu\nu}\ I^{R}_{2};\qquad I^{R}_{0\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{4}g_{\mu\nu}\ I^{R}_{0},

(133)

with the definitions,

\displaystyle\mu_{M}^{2}=\mu_{s}^{2}+M^{2},

\displaystyle M_{c}^{2}\equiv\lim_{N,M_{R}\to\infty}\left(\frac{M_{R}^{2}}{\ln N}\right),

\displaystyle\varepsilon(x)=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-)^{n-1}}{n\ n!}x^{n}=\int_{0}^{x}d\sigma\frac{1-e^{-\sigma}}{\sigma}.

(134)

Where the primary regulator mass
M_{R}
is taken to be infinity so as to recover the original integrals and the regulator number
N
is set to be infinity so as to make the regularized theory independent of the regularization prescription.
\gamma_{E}
is the Euler constant (
\gamma_{E}=0.577215\cdots
) and
\varepsilon(x)
is a special function with
x=\mu_{M}^{2}/M_{c}^{2}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}(x)\equiv\partial_{x}\varepsilon(x)
. It is noted that
M_{c}
and
\mu_{s}
are regarded as intrinsic mass scales. In general, the mass scale
M_{c}
can always be made to be finite as long as the ratio
M_{R}^{2}/\ln N
is kept as a finite quantity when the primary regulator mass
M_{R}
and regulator number
N
are approaching to infinity.

It is seen that the LORE method truly brings on an infinity-free and symmetry-preserving regularization method with the presence of two intrinsic mass scales and the satisifaction of symmetry invariance consistency conditions. Specifically,
M_{c}
provides an ultraviolet (UV) ‘cutoff’, and
\mu_{s}
sets an infrared (IR) ‘cutoff’ for massless case
M^{2}=0
. It is clear that the LORE method does maintain the original divergent structure of integrals when taking
M_{c}\to\infty
and
\mu_{s}\to 0
. In comparison to the dimensional regularization (DR) scheme, although the DR scheme can also result in the same symmetry invariance consistency conditions to preserve gauge invariance, while its regularized quadratic divergent ILIs are actually suppressed to be a logarithmic divergence multiplying by the mass scale
{\cal M}^{2}
or vanishes (
I^{R}_{2}=0
) for the massless case
{\cal M}^{2}=0
. This is because the dimensional regularization scheme modifies the original theory by making an analytical extension for the space-time dimensions of original theory, which makes the DR scheme to be limited in directly applying to theories which require to keep well-defined spacetime dimensions and maintain quadratic divergent structure, such as chiral type theories and supersymmetric theories as well as theories with dynamically spontaneous symmetry breaking.

Furthermore, the LORE method becomes simple at one loop level and gets very applicable at high loop level for understanding the overlapping divergence structure of Feynman diagrams. Nevertheless, LORE method is not motivated for working out a much simpler regularization scheme but for figuring out an infinity-free and symmetry-preserving regularization method without modifying the original theory. It has been demonstrated in
HW1
;
HLW
that the derivation of ILIs from Feynman integrals by adopting UVDP parametrization brings about the so-called Bjorken-Drell’s circuit analogy between Feynman diagrams and electric circuits
BD
, which enables us to pick up one-to-one correspondence between the divergences of UVDP parameters and the subdiagrams of Feynman diagrams.

Therefore, the LORE method does overcome the shortages and limitations appearing in some widely-used regularization schemes. In fact, the LORE method has been applied to make numerous interesting applications and carry out various consistent calculations
Cui:2008uv
;
Cui:2008bk
;
Ma:2005md
;
Ma:2006yc
;
cui:2011
;
DW
;
HW
;
Tang:2008ah
;
Tang:2010cr
;
Tang2011
, such as: how it preserves non-Abelian gauge symmetry
Cui:2008uv
and supersymmetry
Cui:2008bk
, how it results in consistent analyses on the chiral anomaly
Ma:2005md
and radiatively induced Lorentz and CPT-violating Chern-Simons term in the extended QED
Ma:2006yc
as well as QED trace anomaly
cui:2011
, how it enables us to derive the gap equation for the dynamically generated spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking at low energy QCD and obtain reliably the dynamic quark masses and mass spectra of light scalar and pseudoscalar mesons in the chiral effective field theory
DW
, and meanwhile understand the chiral symmetry restoration in chiral thermodynamic model
HW
. Especially, the LORE method has been utilized to perform the consistent computations on the quantum gravitational contribution to gauge theories and obtain asymptotic free power-law running
Tang:2008ah
;
Tang:2010cr
;
Tang2011
, and also to clarify the important issues raised in
HGG
for the Higgs decay process
H\to\gamma\gamma
HTW
. It is intriguing to observe that the LORE method has also been adopted to achieve a dynamical spontaneous symmetry breaking mechanism for the electroweak symmetry in the SM and arrive at a sliding electroweak symmetry breaking scale to be around
\mu_{EW}\sim 760
GeV
BCW
. In general, the LORE method was proved to be applicable to theories in high dimensional spacetime
Chapling1
;
Chapling2
.

With the above analyses, we should turn to make discussions on the finiteness and renormalizability of GQFT. The crucial point in LORE is the appearance of two intrinsic energy scales
M_{c}
and
\mu_{s}
which play the role as ultraviolet (UV) cut-off and infrared (IR) cut-off of loop integrals to avoid infinities in QFT without spoiling symmetries of original theory. These two energy scales should get physically meaningful as the characteristic energy scale
M_{c}
and sliding energy scale
\mu_{s}
indicated from the so-called folk’s theorem
SW1
;
SW2
addressed by Weinberg. Such a folk’s theorem states that: any quantum theory that at sufficiently low energy and large distances looks Lorentz invariant and satisfies the cluster decomposition principle will also at sufficiently low energy look like a quantum field theory. It implies that there must exist a characteristic energy scale (CES)
M_{c}
which makes the statement of sufficiently low energy to be reliable. It also means that such a CES
M_{c}
can be either a fundamental mass scale for a basic theory or a dynamically generated energy scale for an effective theory. In the GQFT, the CES
M_{c}
is considered to be a fundamental mass scale, which distinguishes to the QFT in Minkowski spacetime. The reason is that the presence of gravigauge field in GQFT will bring about the scaling gauge invariance of the action, which always allows us to set up a fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
by imposing a suitable scaling gauge fixing condition. Such a fundamental mass scale
M_{\kappa}
is naturally identified to the CES
M_{c}
for characterizing the ultraviolet behavior of the theory, i.e.:

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}\simeq M_{c},

(135)

which makes any theory in the framework of GQFT to be infinity-free. Namely, there exists in principle no divergences in GQFT and any quantum contribution from high dimensionful interactions in GQFT should be finite and physically meaningful.

In addition to the above consideration, let us follow along the key concept of biframe spacetime in GQFT to understand the finiteness and renormalizability of GQFT. In general, the biframe spacetime forms the fiber bundle structure with the base spacetime taken to be a globally flat Minkowski spacetime and the fiber bringing on a locally flat gravigauge spacetime. As the base spacetime acts as an inertial reference frame for describing the motion of basic fields, the energy momentum of basic fields in such a base spacetime can approach to be infinite large and small as it is considered to be a continue and differential spacetime to ensure the laws of energy momentum conservation, which becomes the main reason why the path integral in such a globally flat Minkowski spacetime gets divergences as the virtual particles can in principle have very short wavelength/high frequency fluctuations or infinite large energy momentum
k_{\mu}\to\infty
. In contrast, the fiber as a locally flat gravigauge spacetime emerges as a non-commutative geometry characterized by the field strength of gravigauge field and reflects the gravitational interaction, which functions as an interaction frame of basic fields. Therefore, the actual energy momentum of all basic fields in such a locally flat gravigauge spacetime is always associated to the dual gravigauge field and presented explicitly as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{a}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}(x)k_{\mu}\sim\int[dq]e^{-iq_{\mu}x^{\mu}}\hat{k}_{a}(q),

\displaystyle\hat{k}_{a}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}(q)k_{\mu},

(136)

which provides a physically meaningful energy momentum in GQFT. For convenience, we may refer to
\hat{k}_{a}
(or
\mathcal{K}_{a}
) as
gravigauging enengy momentum
. Unlike the globally flat Minkowski spacetime, the locally flat gravigauge spacetime as a matter field spacetime with noncommutative geometry is in principle no longer to appear as an infinite continue spacetime, so the gravigauging energy momentum
\hat{k}_{a}
(or
\mathcal{K}_{a}
) should not approach to be an infinite large/small quantity even when the ordinary energy momentum
k_{\mu}
in globally flat Minkowski spacetime goes to be infinite, i.e.:

\displaystyle\hat{k}_{a}\neq\infty,\qquad k_{\mu}\to\infty,

(137)

which may be comprehended as follows: once at a short distance with infinite large energy momentum
k_{\mu}\to\infty
in the base spacetime of coordinates, the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
will become infinite large
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}\to\infty
and its dual/inverse gravigauge field
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
gets infinitely small
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\to 0
, whereas the gravigauging energy momentum
\hat{k}_{a}
(or
\mathcal{K}_{a}
) as a combination quantity
\hat{k}_{a}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}(q)k_{\mu}
(or
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}(x)k_{\mu}
) remains to keep a physically meaningful finite energy momentum. Therefore, it appears in principle no infinite gravigauging energy momentum in GQFT. On the other hand, there should exist a potential horizon
L_{H}
for the locally flat gravigauge spacetime as a matter field spacetime, which can provide a natural infrared cutoff scale, i.e.:

\displaystyle\mu_{s}\to 1/L_{H},

(138)

which allows us to remove the infrared divergence in GQFT.

From the above analyses and discussions, we are in the position to conclude that any divergence should in principle disappear in GQFT, which provides a resonable answer to the criticisms raised by Dirac and Feynman about the treatment of infinities and divergences via the usual renormalization schemes in QFT. Actually, the globally flat Minkowski spacetime as an inertial frame of free motion fields is unobservable, only the locally flat gravigauge spacetime as a gravitational frame of dynamic interaction fields becomes observable, which constitutes our observed universe. Moreover, by applying for the concept of renormalization group developed in refs.
KGW
;
GML
, we should be able to study physical phenomena at any interesting energy scale by integrating out physical effects at higher energy scale, and define a finite renormalized theory at any interesting renormalization scale. In fact, the existence of both the characteristic energy scale (CES)
M_{c}\simeq M_{\kappa}
and sliding energy scale(SES)
\mu_{s}\sim 1/L_{H}
in GQFT allows us to choose the renormalization scale at any scale of interest between them. In this sense, GQFT becomes more natural to provide a profound theoretical framework for describing all basic forces.

VII
Gravitational relativistic quantum theory on Dirac spinor field

The above various formalisms of the action enable us to derive the equations of motion for all basic fields based on the least action principle. These equations can be applied to investigate the dynamics of basic fields in the gravitational relativistic quantum theory. Let us first study the equation of motion for the chirality-based Dirac spinor
\Psi_{-}(x)
in the presence of gravitational interaction and spin gauge interaction as well as electromagnetic interaction, which generalizes the usual relativistic quantum mechanics to gravitational relativistic quantum theory.

VII.1
Generalized Dirac equation in gravitational relativistic quantum theory

Following along the least action principle, we are able to arrive at the following two types of the equations of motion for the chirality-based Dirac spinor field:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{-a}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;\;a}i\left({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathbf{V}}_{\nu}\right)\Psi_{-}(x)-\frac{m}{M_{\kappa}}\Sigma_{-}^{6}\Psi_{-}(x)=0,

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{-a}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}i\left({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}\right)\varPsi_{-}(x)-\frac{m}{M_{\kappa}}\phi(x)\Sigma_{-}^{6}\varPsi_{-}(x)=0,

(139)

which correspond to the scaling gauge invariant dimensionless fields and local scaling charged fields. Where we have introduced the following vector-like field:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{V}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{A}\,\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}(\hat{\mathfrak{A}}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;\;b})=-\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{A}_{\mu a}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{ab},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\chi\,\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}(\hat{\chi}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;b})=-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mu a}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}\mathsf{A}_{\nu}^{ab},

(140)

which is given by the gravigauge field and spin covariant gauge field. Such a vector-like field preserves the scaling gauge invariance for the equation of motion, which can be verified explicitly from the second equation in Eq.(
VII.1
) when all fields are represented as local scaling charged fields. For convenience, we may refer to such a vector-like field as a graviscaling induced gauge field, which is distinguished from the ordinary gauge field of Lie group due to the associated imaginary factor in the covariant derivative.

For simplicity of discussions, let us consider the equation of motion of chirality-based Dirac spinor by taking the scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein basis, i.e.:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\Sigma_{-a}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}i\left({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}\right)\varPsi_{-}(x)-m\Sigma_{-}^{6}\varPsi_{-}(x)=0.

(141)

In terms of the vector-like spinor representation of Dirac spinor field in four-dimensional Hilbert space, the above equation can be simplified into the following form:

\displaystyle\gamma^{a}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}i\left({\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu}\right)\psi-m\psi=0,

(142)

with the covariant derivative defined as follows:

\displaystyle i({\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu})\equiv i\partial_{\mu}+\frac{1}{2}g_{G}i\chi_{\mu a}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}\mathsf{A}_{\nu}^{ab}+(\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu}^{bc}+g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{bc})\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{bc}+g_{E}A_{\mu}.

(143)

Let us now investigate the quadratic form of the equation of motion for the Dirac spinor in the presence of gravitational interaction and spin gauge interaction as well as electromagnetic interaction. Its explicit form is found to be:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}(\nabla_{\mu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu})({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu})\psi+m^{2}\psi

\displaystyle\;\;=\Sigma^{cd}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{d}^{\;\;\nu}[{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{ab}+g_{E}F_{\mu\nu}-g_{G}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}i({\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\rho})-i{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu\nu}\,]\psi,

(144)

with the following definitions:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu})\equiv{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu})-\varGamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}({\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\rho}),

\displaystyle\varGamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}=\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}(\partial_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu\,b}^{a}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;b}\,)\equiv\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}+g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}\chi_{\nu b}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\equiv{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}-{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}\equiv\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}\chi_{\nu b}-\mathsf{A}_{\nu}^{ab}\chi_{\mu b},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu\nu}\equiv\partial_{\mu}{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu}.

(145)

Where
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}
and
F_{\mu\nu}
are spin gauge field strength and electromagnetic field strength, respectively.
\Gamma_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
denotes the affine connection and
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu\nu}
represents the graviscaling induced gauge field strength.

It is intriguing to notice that although the gauge invariant action is hermitian, while the equation of motion of Dirac spinor field gets an emergent dissipative term characterized by the graviscaling induced gauge field
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu}
and field strength
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu\nu}
due to the presence of gravitational interaction and spin gauge interaction via the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}
and spin covariant gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}
.

To display explicitly the effects of gravitational interaction and spin gauge interaction on the dynamics of Dirac fermion, we represent the above equation of motion into the following form:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}({\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu})({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu})\psi+\left(\partial_{\mu}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}+2\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\partial_{\mu}\ln\chi+g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\nu}\right)({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu})\psi

\displaystyle+(m^{2}+\frac{1}{16}R+\frac{1}{16}g_{G}\mathsf{F}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\nu}-\frac{1}{8}g_{G}\epsilon_{abcd}\mathsf{F}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\hat{\chi}^{c\mu}\hat{\chi}^{d\nu}i\gamma_{5})\psi

\displaystyle\;=\varSigma^{cb}[\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}(g_{E}F_{\mu\nu}-i{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu\nu})+\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\mu}\left(ig_{G}\mathsf{F}_{\mu\nu b}^{a}-2g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{\mu b}^{a}i({\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}-{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu})\right)\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\nu}\,]\psi,

(146)

where we have used the algebra relation of
\gamma
-matrices:

\displaystyle\Sigma^{cd}\Sigma^{ab}=\frac{1}{2}i(\Sigma^{cb}\eta^{ad}-\Sigma^{db}\eta^{ac}-\Sigma^{ca}\eta^{db}+\Sigma^{da}\eta^{cb})+\frac{1}{16}(\eta^{ca}\eta^{db}-\eta^{da}\eta^{cb})+\frac{1}{4}\epsilon^{abcd}i\gamma_{5},

(147)

and the properties of Riemann and Ricci curvature tensors:

\displaystyle R_{\mu\rho\nu\sigma}+R_{\rho\nu\mu\sigma}+R_{\nu\mu\rho\sigma}=0,\quad R_{\mu\nu}=R_{\nu\mu}.

(148)

VII.2
Gravigauge Dirac equation in locally flat gravigauge spacetime

From the gauge and scaling invariant action presented in Eqs.(
80
)-(
85
) and (
88
), it is noticed that the Dirac fermion actually couples to the spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{c}^{ab}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}
defined in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. Therefore, it is particularly interesting to derive the equation of motion of Dirac spinor field in locally flat gravigauge spacetime via a hidden coordinate formalism. Taking the scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein basis, we can rewrite the equation of motion for the Dirac spinor into the following simple form:

\displaystyle\gamma^{c}i\left({\mathcal{D}}_{c}-{\mathsf{V}}_{c}\right)\psi-m\psi=0,

(149)

which appears formally to be analogous to the Dirac equation in globally flat Minkowski spacetime. Whereas they are essentially distinguished due to the presence of spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{c}^{ab}
and the emergent non-commutation relation of covariant derivative operator in locally flat gravigauge spacetime shown in Eq.(
V.2
), which can be seen from the following definitions and relations:

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{c}\equiv i\eth_{c}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{c}^{ab}\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{ab}+g_{E}A_{a},

\displaystyle[\eth_{c}\,,\eth_{d}]=\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cd]}^{a}\mspace{1mu}\eth_{a},\quad\eth_{c}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\mu}\partial_{\mu},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{c}\equiv\frac{1}{2}g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{bc}^{b}\equiv\frac{1}{2}g_{G}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}\mathsf{A}_{\nu c}^{b}.

(150)

To be more explicit, let us check the covariant quadratic form for the equation of motion in locally flat gravigauge spacetime:

\displaystyle(\nabla_{c}-{\mathsf{V}}_{c})({\mathcal{D}}^{c}-{\mathsf{V}}^{c})\psi+m^{2}\psi

\displaystyle=\Sigma^{cd}[{\mathcal{F}}_{cd}^{\,ab}\frac{1}{2}\Sigma_{ab}+g_{E}F_{cd}-g_{G}{\mathbf{F}}_{cd}^{a}i({\mathcal{D}}_{a}-{\mathsf{V}}_{a})-i{\mathsf{V}}_{cd}\,]\psi,

(151)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\nabla_{c}({\mathcal{D}}_{d}-{\mathsf{V}}_{d})\equiv{\mathcal{D}}_{c}({\mathcal{D}}_{d}-{\mathsf{V}}_{d})-{\mathcal{A}}_{cd}^{a}({\mathcal{D}}_{a}-{\mathsf{V}}_{a}),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{F}}_{cd}^{a}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[cd]}^{a}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cd]}^{a}\equiv\mathsf{A}_{[cd]}^{a}\equiv\mathsf{A}_{cd}^{a}-\mathsf{A}_{dc}^{a},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}_{cd}\equiv\eth_{c}{\mathsf{V}}_{d}-\eth_{d}{\mathsf{V}}_{c}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cd]}^{a}{\mathsf{V}}_{a}.

(152)

Where all the gauge fields and field strengths are defined in locally flat gravigauge spacetime as shown in Eqs.(
V.2
) and (
V.2
). It can be further demonstrated that Eq.(
VII.2
) can be rewritten into the following explicit form in locally flat gravigauge spacetime:

\displaystyle[({\mathcal{D}}_{c}-{\mathsf{V}}_{c})({\mathcal{D}}^{c}-{\mathsf{V}}^{c})+(\partial_{\mu}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\mu}+\eth_{c}\ln\chi+\mathsf{A}_{a}^{ac})({\mathcal{D}}^{c}-{\mathsf{V}}^{c})]\psi

\displaystyle+(m^{2}+\frac{1}{16}R+\frac{1}{16}g_{G}\mathsf{F}_{ab}^{\,ba}-\frac{1}{8}g_{G}\epsilon^{cdab}\mathsf{F}_{cdab}i\gamma_{5})\psi

\displaystyle=\varSigma^{cb}[g_{E}F_{cb}-2g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{c\,b}^{a}i({\mathcal{D}}_{a}-{\mathsf{V}}_{a})+ig_{G}\mathsf{F}_{ca\mspace{1mu}b}^{a}-i{\mathsf{V}}_{cb})\,]\psi.

(153)

It is interesting to notice that the Ricci curvature scalar
R\equiv R_{cb}\eta^{cb}\equiv R_{\mu\sigma}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\sigma}
and the spin covariant gauge field strength scalar
\mathsf{F}_{cd}^{ab}\eta_{b}^{\;c}\eta_{a}^{\;d}\equiv\mathsf{F}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\nu}
emerge as mass-like terms in the quadratic form of equation of motion for the Dirac fermion, which indicates that a non-zero Ricci curvature scalar
R\neq 0
appears to generate an effective mass for the Dirac fermion when it propagates in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. Meanwhile, the totally antisymmetric tensor
\epsilon^{cdab}\mathsf{F}_{cdab}
arising from the spin covariant gauge field strength behaves as a pseudoscalar.

In conclusion, the equation of motion of Dirac spinor field in the presence of gravitational interaction and spin gauge interaction characterized by the spin gravigauge field (or gravigauge field) and spin gauge field generalizes the relativistic quantum mechanics described by Dirac equation in globally flat Minkowski spacetime to obtain a gravitational relativistic quantum theory in locally flat gravigauge spacetime with the emergence of non-commutative geometry.

VIII
Gravidynamics with gauge-type and geometric gravitational equations as extension to Einstein theory of general relativity

To study the dynamics of gauge fields, we are going to take the gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;\;a}(x)\equiv\phi(x)\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}(x)
and spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}(x)
as independent degrees of freedom based on the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry WS(1,3). In particular, we will discuss the potential new effects of gravidynamics beyond the Einstein theory of general relativity.

VIII.1
Gravidynamics with gauge-type gravitational equations of gravigauge field

To describe the gravidynamics, let us derive the equation of motion for the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field as gravigauge field. The gauge covariant and scaling invariant equations of motion in correspondence to the scaling gauge invariant gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
and scaling charged gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
are found to be:

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{a}=\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

\displaystyle\tilde{d}_{\nu}\tilde{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{a}={\mathcal{J}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

(154)

where the field strengths
\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{a}
and
\tilde{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{a}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{a}\equiv M_{\kappa}^{-1}\mathfrak{A}\mspace{1mu}\tilde{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},\quad\mathsf{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\equiv\partial_{\mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;a}-\partial_{\nu}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;a},

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{a}\equiv\chi\mspace{1mu}M_{\kappa}^{-2}\phi^{2}\tilde{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\mathrm{F}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},\quad\mathrm{F}_{\mu\nu}^{a}\equiv d_{\mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}-d_{\nu}\chi_{\mu}^{\;a},

\displaystyle\tilde{d}_{\mu}\equiv\partial_{\mu}-S_{\mu},\quad d_{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}+S_{\mu},\quad S_{\mu}\equiv\partial_{\mu}\ln\phi,

(155)

and the currents
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
and
{\mathcal{J}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
are given explicitly by:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 16\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},\quad\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\equiv{\mathbf{J}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+\widetilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\rho}\mathsf{F}_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{c}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}\mspace{1mu}\mathsf{F}_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{\mathsf{F}}^{\rho\nu}_{c},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{J}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}\mathfrak{A}\mspace{1mu}\{(\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\rho}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c}^{\;\mu}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c}^{\;\rho})(\bar{\Psi}_{-}\varSigma_{-}^{c}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}\Psi_{-}+H.c.)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}\frac{m}{M_{\kappa}}\bar{\Psi}_{-}\Gamma^{6}\Psi_{-}-g_{E}^{-2}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\rho\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathbf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle M_{\kappa}\mathfrak{A}\mspace{1mu}\{-g_{G}^{-2}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\rho\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu})\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}{\mathcal{F}}_{\rho\nu}^{bc}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}bc}

(156)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}(\bar{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{\mathsf{H}}^{[\rho\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}){\mathcal{F}}_{\rho\nu}^{b}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}),

and

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 16\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+\tilde{{\mathsf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},\quad\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\equiv{\mathsf{J}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+\widetilde{{\mathsf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

\displaystyle\tilde{{\mathsf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}\mathrm{F}_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{\mathrm{F}}^{\mu\nu}_{c}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}\mspace{1mu}\mathrm{F}_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{\mathrm{F}}^{\rho\nu}_{c},

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi\mspace{1mu}\{(\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}-\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\rho})(\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\varSigma_{-}^{c}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}\varPsi_{-}+H.c.)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\frac{m}{M_{\kappa}}\phi\bar{\varPsi}_{-}\Gamma^{6}\varPsi_{-}-(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu})\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathsf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi\mspace{1mu}\{-(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu})\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}{\mathcal{F}}_{\rho\nu}^{bc}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}bc}

(157)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}\phi^{2}(\bar{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu})({\mathbf{F}}_{\rho\nu}^{b}+S_{\rho\nu}^{b})({\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}+S_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}})\}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}}{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\left(\chi\,\bar{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\phi({\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}+S_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}})\right).

In the above equations, we have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}})=\partial_{\nu}(\mathfrak{A}\,\bar{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}})-\mathfrak{A}\,g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu a}^{b}\bar{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}},\quad\tilde{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\tilde{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\chi}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\chi}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}},\quad\tilde{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\,\mu}\hat{\chi}_{d}^{\;\,\nu}\hat{\chi}_{c^{\prime}}^{\;\,\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{d^{\prime}}^{\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\tilde{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}-\bar{\eta}^{dcc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}),\quad\tilde{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\tilde{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}-\tilde{\eta}^{dcc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}).

(158)

Taking the scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein basis, we arrive at the following gauge-type gravitational equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}_{a}=J_{a}^{\;\mu},

(159)

where the field strength
\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}_{a}
and current
J_{a}^{\;\mu}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}_{a}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\chi\mspace{1mu}\tilde{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

(160)

and

\displaystyle J_{a}^{\;\mu}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 16\pi G_{N}\hat{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+\tilde{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},\quad\hat{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\equiv\mathrm{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+\widetilde{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

\displaystyle\tilde{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}F_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}_{c}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{F}^{\rho\nu}_{c},

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi\mspace{1mu}\{(\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}-\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\rho})\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\gamma^{c}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}\psi+H.c.)

\displaystyle+
