\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}m\bar{\psi}\psi-(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu})\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\},

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi\mspace{1mu}\{-(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu})\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}{\mathcal{F}}_{\rho\nu}^{bc}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}bc}

(161)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{G}^{2}(\bar{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}){\mathbf{F}}_{\rho\nu}^{b}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle m_{G}^{2}{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}(\chi\,\bar{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}),

with the conserved current,

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}J_{a}^{\;\mu}=0.

(162)

Such equations of motion for gravigauge field are obtained to describe the gravidynamics and referred to as gauge-type gravitational equations.

VIII.2
Gauge-type gravidynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime

From the gauge and scaling invariant action built in hidden coordinate formalisms in Eqs.(
80
)-(
85
) and (
88
), it is seen that all interactions emerge in locally flat gravigauge spacetime spanned by the gravigauge bases. It should be appropriate to derive the gauge-type gravitational equation in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. When taking the scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein basis, we arrive at the following equation of motion:

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{d}\tilde{F}^{cd}_{b}=J_{b}^{\;\;c},

(163)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{cd}_{a}\equiv\tilde{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}F_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},\quad F_{cd}^{a}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{d}^{\;\;\nu}F_{\mu\nu}^{a},

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{d}\tilde{F}^{cd}_{b}\equiv\eth_{d}\tilde{F}^{cd}_{b}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{da}^{c}\tilde{F}^{ad}_{b}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{da}^{d}\tilde{F}^{ca}_{b},

(164)

for the field strength and covariant derivative, and

\displaystyle J_{b}^{\;\;c}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle 16\pi G_{N}\hat{J}_{b}^{\;\;c}+\tilde{J}_{b}^{\;c},\quad\hat{J}_{b}^{\;c}\equiv\mathrm{J}_{b}^{\;c}+\widetilde{J}_{b}^{\;c},

\displaystyle\tilde{J}_{b}^{\;c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{bd}^{a}\tilde{F}^{cd}_{a}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{b}^{\;c}\mspace{1mu}F_{cd}^{a}\tilde{F}^{cd}_{a},

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}_{b}^{\;c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\gamma_{c}i{\mathcal{D}}_{b}\psi+H.c.)-\eta_{b}^{\;c}[\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\gamma^{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{a}\psi+H.c.)-m\bar{\psi}\psi]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(\eta_{b}^{\;a}\eta^{cc^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{b}^{\;c}\eta^{ac^{\prime}})\eta^{dd^{\prime}}F_{ad}F_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{J}_{b}^{\;c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\eta_{b}^{\;a}\eta^{cc^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{b}^{\;c}\eta^{ac^{\prime}})\eta^{dd^{\prime}}F_{ad}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}F_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}

(165)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{G}^{2}(\eta_{b}^{\;c^{\prime\prime}}\eta_{b^{\prime}}^{\;c}\bar{\eta}^{[b^{\prime}d]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{b}^{\;c}\bar{\eta}^{[c^{\prime\prime}d]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}){\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime\prime}d}^{a}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle m_{G}^{2}\bar{{\mathcal{D}}}_{d}(\bar{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}),

for the currents. The gauge covariant derivative in the last term is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{D}}}_{d}(\bar{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\eth_{d}(\bar{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}})+(\mathsf{\Omega}_{da}^{c}\bar{\eta}^{[ad]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{da}^{d}\bar{\eta}^{[ca]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}-g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{db}^{a}\bar{\eta}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}){\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}.

(166)

Such equations of motion characterize the gauge-type gravidynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

VIII.3
Geometric gravidynamics and extension to Einstein theory of general relativity

To obtain geometric gravitational equations, let us take into account the gauge-gravity and gravity-geometry correspondences shown in Eqs.(
94
)-(
95
) and (
98
). When ignoring the total derivative term, the least action principle leads to the following relations:

\displaystyle 2\mathbf{R}_{\rho\sigma}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\rho}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\sigma}-\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}\mathbf{R}=-16\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

\displaystyle 2\phi^{2}\mathsf{R}_{\rho\sigma}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\sigma}-\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}\phi^{2}\mathsf{R}=-16\pi G_{N}M_{\kappa}^{2}\mspace{1mu}\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

(167)

with

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{\rho\sigma}\equiv R_{\rho\sigma}-6\partial_{\rho}\ln\phi\partial_{\sigma}\ln\phi,

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}\equiv\hat{\chi}^{\rho\sigma}\mathsf{R}_{\rho\sigma}=R-6\hat{\chi}^{\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}\ln\phi\partial_{\sigma}\ln\phi.

(168)

In the Einstein basis, we can rewrite the above relation into the following simple form:

\displaystyle R_{a}^{\;\mu}-\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}R=-8\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}\hat{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},

(169)

with

\displaystyle R_{a}^{\;\mu}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a^{\prime}}^{\;\nu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{b^{\prime}}^{\;\mu}R_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}b^{\prime}}.

(170)

When projecting the tensor and current in biframe spacetime into the base spacetime of coordinates by applying for the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}
), we arrive at the following two geometric gravitational equations:

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mu\nu}R+8\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}\widetilde{T}_{\mu\nu}=-8\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}T_{\mu\nu},

(171)

and

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\;\,[\mu\nu]}^{\rho}+\widetilde{T}_{[\mu\nu]}=-m_{G}^{-2}T_{[\mu\nu]}.

(172)

The above two equations correspond to the symmetric tensor and antisymmetric tensor in the base spacetime of coordinates.

The geometric gravitational equation presented in Eq.(
171
) brings on the extension to Einstein theory of general relativity in the presence of spin gauge field, which is characterized by the symmetric tensor
\widetilde{T}_{\mu\nu}
. The explicit forms of the tensors are given as follows:

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}+\widetilde{T}_{\mu\nu}=T_{\nu\mu}+\widetilde{T}_{\nu\mu}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}(\chi_{\mu\rho}\hat{J}_{a}^{\;\rho}\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}+\chi_{\nu\rho}\hat{J}_{a}^{\;\rho}\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}),

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{4}[\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\psi+\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\psi+H.c.]

\displaystyle\qquad-\chi_{\mu\nu}[\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\gamma^{c}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\rho}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}\psi+H.c.)-m\bar{\psi}\psi]

\displaystyle\qquad-(\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}})\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\sigma}F_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{\mu\nu}=-(\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}})\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}{\mathcal{F}}_{\rho\sigma}^{ab}{\mathcal{F}}_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}ab}+m_{G}^{2}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\;\,(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\qquad+m_{G}^{2}[\frac{1}{2}(\chi_{\mu\lambda}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho}+\chi_{\nu\lambda}\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho})\bar{\chi}^{[\lambda\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}_{\mu\nu}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}]{\mathbf{F}}_{\rho\sigma}^{a}{\mathbf{F}}_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

(173)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\;\,(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}\equiv\partial_{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}-\Gamma_{\rho\lambda}^{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\lambda\nu}^{\rho}+\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\lambda\mu}^{\rho}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}),

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\chi_{\mu\sigma}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}\equiv\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}+\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[\mu\nu]}^{\rho},

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}+\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\nu\mu}^{\rho})=\frac{1}{2}(\chi_{\mu\sigma}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}+\chi_{\nu\sigma}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a})\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}.

(174)

Where
T_{\mu\nu}
is the usual four energy momentum tensor arising from the Dirac fermion and electromagnetic gauge field in the presence of spin gauge field and gravigauge field, and
\widetilde{T}_{\mu\nu}
is purely attributed to the inhomogeneous spin gauge field governed by the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry.

The geometric gravitational equation given in Eq.(
172
) is obtained from the antisymmetric tensors, which indicates that the gauge invariant field strength
\tilde{\mathsf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}
is governed by the relevant antisymmetric tensors
T_{[\mu\nu]}
and
\widetilde{T}_{[\mu\nu]}
. Their explicit forms are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\;\,[\mu\nu]}^{\rho}\equiv\partial_{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[\mu\nu]}^{\rho}-\Gamma_{\rho\lambda}^{\rho}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[\mu\nu]}^{\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\lambda\nu}^{\rho}-\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\lambda\mu}^{\rho}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}-g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}),

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[\mu\nu]}^{\rho}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}-\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\nu\mu}^{\rho})=\frac{1}{2}(\chi_{\mu\sigma}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}-\chi_{\nu\sigma}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a})\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{[\mu\nu]}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\chi_{\mu\lambda}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho}-\chi_{\nu\lambda}\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho})\bar{\chi}^{[\lambda\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\rho\sigma}^{a}{\mathbf{F}}_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

\displaystyle T_{[\mu\nu]}\equiv\frac{1}{4}\left(\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\psi-\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\psi+H.c\right),

(175)

which reflect the basic feature of gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}
as the fundamental gravitational field instead of the symmetric gravimetric field
\chi_{\mu\nu}
.

VIII.4
Geometric gravidynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime

Let us now project all tensors and currents into the ones in locally flat gravigauge spacetime by adopting the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}
) as Goldstone-like boson. The following gravitational equations are obtained:

\displaystyle R_{cb}-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{cb}R+8\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}\widetilde{T}_{cb}=-8\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}T_{cb},

(176)

and

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{D}}}_{d}\bar{\mathsf{F}}_{[cb]}^{d}+\widetilde{T}_{[cb]}=-m_{G}^{-2}T_{[cb]},

(177)

where the tensors are given by the field strength of spin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{c}^{ab}
:

\displaystyle R_{cb}=\eta^{da}R_{cdab},\quad R=\eta^{cb}R_{cb},

\displaystyle R_{cdab}=\eth_{c}\mathsf{\Omega}_{dab}-\eth_{d}\mathsf{\Omega}_{cab}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{cae}\mathsf{\Omega}_{db}^{e}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{dae}\mathsf{\Omega}_{cb}^{e}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cd]}^{e}\mathsf{\Omega}_{eab}.

(178)

The symmetric tensors
T_{cb}
and
\widetilde{T}_{cb}
are provided by the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle T_{cb}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}[\bar{\psi}\gamma_{c}i{\mathcal{D}}_{b}\psi+\bar{\psi}\gamma_{b}i{\mathcal{D}}_{c}\psi+H.c.]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\eta_{cb}[\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\gamma^{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{a}\psi+H.c.)-m\bar{\psi}\psi]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(\eta_{c}^{\;a}\eta_{b}^{\;c^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{cb}\eta^{ac^{\prime}})\eta^{dd^{\prime}}F_{ad}F_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{cb}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\eta_{c}^{\;c^{\prime\prime}}\eta_{b}^{\;c^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{cb}\eta^{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime}})\eta^{dd^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{c^{\prime\prime}d}^{ab^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}ab^{\prime}}+m_{G}^{2}\bar{{\mathcal{D}}}_{d}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\;(cb)}^{d}

(179)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle m_{G}^{2}[\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{cb^{\prime}}\eta_{b}^{\;c^{\prime\prime}}+\eta_{bb^{\prime}}\eta_{c}^{\;c^{\prime\prime}})\bar{\eta}^{[b^{\prime}d]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\eta_{cb}\bar{\eta}^{[c^{\prime\prime}d]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}]{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime\prime}d}^{a}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{D}}}_{d}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\;(cb)}^{d}\equiv\eth_{d}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(cb)}^{d}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{da}^{d}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(cb)}^{a}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{\Omega}_{dc}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ab}^{d}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{db}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ac}^{d}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{db}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ca}^{d}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{dc}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ba}^{d}),

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{cb}^{d}\equiv\eta_{cb^{\prime}}\eta_{b}^{\;a}\bar{\eta}^{[db^{\prime}]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}\equiv\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(cb)}^{d}+\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[cb]}^{d},

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{(cb)}^{d}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{cb}^{d}+\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{bc}^{d})=\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{cb^{\prime}}\eta_{b}^{\;a}+\eta_{bb^{\prime}}\bar{\eta}_{c}^{\;a})\bar{\eta}^{[db^{\prime}]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

(180)

The antisymmetric tensors are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{D}}}_{d}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{\;[cb]}^{d}\equiv\eth_{d}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[cb]}^{d}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{da}^{d}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[cb]}^{a}-\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{\Omega}_{dc}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ab}^{d}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{db}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ac}^{d}+g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{db}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ca}^{d}-g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{dc}^{a}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{ba}^{d}),

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{[cb]}^{d}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{cb}^{d}-\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{bc}^{d})=\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{cb^{\prime}}\eta_{b}^{\;a}-\eta_{bb^{\prime}}\eta_{c}^{\;a})\bar{\eta}^{[db^{\prime}]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

(181)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{T}_{[cb]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{cb^{\prime}}\eta_{b}^{\;c^{\prime\prime}}-\eta_{bb^{\prime}}\eta_{c}^{\;c^{\prime\prime}})\bar{\eta}^{[b^{\prime}d]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\mathsf{F}_{c^{\prime\prime}d}^{a}\mathsf{F}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

\displaystyle T_{[cb]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}[\bar{\psi}\gamma_{c}i{\mathcal{D}}_{b}\psi-\bar{\psi}\gamma_{b}i{\mathcal{D}}_{c}\psi+H.c.].

(182)

The gravitational equations in Eqs.(
176
) and (
177
) provide the equivalent formalisms to characterize the gravidynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime spanned by the gravigauge bases.

VIII.5
New effects of gravidynamics beyond Einstein theory of general relativity

The gravidynamics described by the geometric gravitational equation presented in Eq.(
171
) or Eq.(
176
) brings about the extension to Einstein theory of general relativity, which is explicitly shown from the additional energy momentum tensor
\widetilde{T}_{\mu\nu}
in coordinate spacetime or
\widetilde{T}_{bc}
in gravigauge spacetime. Such an additional energy momentum tensor arises solely from the inhomogeneous spin gauge field which is governed by the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry.

To explore potential new effects caused from such a gravidynamics beyond the Einstein theory of general relativity, let us turn to investigate the property of spin gauge field. It is interesting to notice from the action shown in Eq.(
88
) that the term given by the spin gauge invariant gravigauge field strength
{\mathbf{F}}_{cd}^{a}
can be rewritten into the following form:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\bar{\eta}^{cdc^{\prime}d^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}m_{G}^{2}{\mathbf{F}}_{cd}^{a}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{4}(\alpha_{G}+\frac{3}{2}\alpha_{W})m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cab]}/g_{G})({\mathcal{A}}^{[cab]}-\mathsf{\Omega}^{[cab]}/g_{G})

(183)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}(\alpha_{G}-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{W})m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{cab}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{cab}/g_{G})({\mathcal{A}}^{cab}-\mathsf{\Omega}^{cab}/g_{G})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}m_{T}^{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cab]}/g_{G})({\mathcal{A}}^{[cab]}-\mathsf{\Omega}^{[cab]}/g_{G})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}m_{S}^{2}({\mathcal{A}}_{(cab]}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{(cab]}/g_{G})({\mathcal{A}}^{(cab]}-\mathsf{\Omega}^{(cab]}/g_{G}),

with

\displaystyle m_{T}^{2}\equiv 2(\alpha_{G}+\alpha_{W})m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2},

\displaystyle m_{S}^{2}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\alpha_{G}-\frac{1}{2}\alpha_{W})m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2}.

(184)

Where we have introduced the following definitions:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{cab}\equiv{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}+{\mathcal{A}}_{(cab]},\quad\mathsf{\Omega}_{cab}\equiv\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cab]}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{(cab]},

(185)

with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{A}}_{cab}+{\mathcal{A}}_{abc}+{\mathcal{A}}_{bac}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{(cab]}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(2{\mathcal{A}}_{cab}-{\mathcal{A}}_{abc}-{\mathcal{A}}_{bac}).

(186)

A similar definition holds for
\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cab]}
and
\mathsf{\Omega}_{(cab]}
. Where
{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}
defines a totally antisymmetric spin gauge field which couples directly to spinor field, and
{\mathcal{A}}_{(cab]}
represents a mixing symmetric spin gauge field.

It indicates that the action of spin gauge invariant gravigauge field strength
{\mathbf{F}}_{cd}^{a}
brings a gauge invariant mass term for the spin gauge field. In general, the totally antisymmetric spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}
as a torsion like field and the mixing symmetric spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{(cab]}
have different masses. Only when the coupling constants satisfy a special relation, they arrive at the same mass, i.e.:

\displaystyle\alpha_{W}=-\frac{2}{3}\alpha_{G},

\displaystyle m_{T}^{2}=m_{S}^{2}=\frac{2}{3}\alpha_{G}m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2}\equiv m_{A}^{2},

(187)

which leads to the mass
m_{A}
for whole spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}
.

As the spin gauge field couples to all spinor fields, i,e., leptons and quarks in the standard model, so that it can in general decay into leptons and quarks when its mass becomes much larger than the masses of those particles. On the other hand, if the spin gauge field is not heavy enough, it should be produced from the colliders of the electron and hadron. As there is no any signal indicated from the experiments on such a spin gauge field, the large hadron collider LHC as the highest energy collider at present provides the most stringent constraint on the mass of spin gauge field up to several TeV for the spin gauge coupling constant being compatible to the electroweak coupling constant. In fact, various tests on the general relativity at short distance arrive at the same order of magnitude though its consistency has well been established in the macroscopic world.

Let us first consider the gravidynamics at the distance
l_{G}
which is larger than the one determined by the mass scale of spin gauge field,
l_{G}>l_{A}=1/m_{A}
. In such a case, the spin gauge field goes away due to its decay into other light particles, so the gravidynamics is characterized by the following simplified equation:

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mu\nu}R+\gamma_{G}\widetilde{R}_{\mu\nu}=-8\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}T_{\mu\nu},

(188)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\gamma_{G}\equiv 8\pi G_{N}m_{G}^{2},

\displaystyle\widetilde{R}_{\mu\nu}=\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{F}_{\;\,(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}+[\frac{1}{2}(\chi_{\mu\lambda}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho}+\chi_{\nu\lambda}\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho})\bar{\chi}^{[\lambda\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}_{\mu\nu}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}]F_{\rho\sigma}^{a}F_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{F}_{\;\,(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}\equiv\partial_{\rho}\bar{F}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}-\Gamma_{\rho\lambda}^{\rho}\bar{F}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\lambda}-\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}\bar{F}_{\lambda\nu}^{\rho}+\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}\bar{F}_{\lambda\mu}^{\rho}),

\displaystyle\bar{F}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\chi_{\mu\sigma}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}F_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},\quad F_{\rho\sigma}^{a}=\partial_{\rho}\chi_{\sigma}^{\;\;a}-\partial_{\sigma}\chi_{\rho}^{\;\;a},

(189)

and

\displaystyle T_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{4}[\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\psi+\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{a}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}\psi+H.c.]

\displaystyle\qquad-\chi_{\mu\nu}[\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\gamma^{c}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\rho}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}\psi+H.c.)-m\bar{\psi}\psi]

\displaystyle\qquad-(\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}})\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\sigma}F_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}},

\displaystyle i{\mathcal{D}}_{\mu}=i\partial_{\mu}+g_{E}A_{\mu},

(190)

where
T_{\mu\nu}
is the usual energy momentum tensor arising from the Dirac fermion and electromagnetic gauge field.

It is seen from the above equation that the new effect beyond Einstein theory of general relativity will arise from the tensor
\widetilde{R}_{\mu\nu}
which is characterized by the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}
. As the spin gauge symmetry always allows us to make the gravigauge field to be symmetric
\chi_{\mu a}=\chi_{a\mu}
by taking the physical unitary gauge, so that its degrees of freedom are the same as those of gravimetric field
\chi_{\mu\nu}
with the relation
\chi_{\mu\nu}=\chi_{\mu a}\eta^{ab}\chi_{b\nu}\equiv(\chi)^{2}_{\mu\nu}
. As the tensor
\widetilde{R}_{\mu\nu}
involves a quadratic derivative over the gravigauge field, it brings on a similar effect as that of Ricci tensor
R_{\mu\nu}
. To be more explicit, let us write down its gauge-type gravitational equation based on Eqs.(
159
)-(
VIII.1
) as follows:

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}_{a}=\tilde{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+\gamma_{G}\check{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}+16\pi G_{N}\mathrm{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

(191)

with

\displaystyle\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}_{a}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\chi\mspace{1mu}\tilde{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\tilde{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\rho}F_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{F}^{\mu\nu}_{c}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\nu}^{c}\tilde{F}^{\rho\nu}_{c},

\displaystyle\check{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi\,(\bar{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{ba^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu})F_{\rho\nu}^{b}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}-\partial_{\nu}(\chi\,\bar{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi\mspace{1mu}\{(\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}-\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\rho})\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\psi}\gamma^{c}i{\mathcal{D}}_{\rho}\psi+H.c.)

(192)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}m\bar{\psi}\psi-(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu})\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}F_{\rho\nu}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\},

where
\check{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
reflects the new effect of gravidynamics beyond Einstein theory of general relativity. It is seen that the usual gravitational current
\tilde{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
and the extra current
\check{J}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
are all described by the gravigauge field strength
F_{\mu\nu}^{a}
.

It is manifest that the magnitude of new effect caused from the gravidynamics based on the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry at a distance larger than
1/m_{G}
is characterized by the mass scale
m_{G}
via the mass ratio
\gamma_{G}
:

\displaystyle\gamma_{G}=8\pi G_{N}m_{G}^{2}=\frac{8\pi m_{G}^{2}}{M_{P}^{2}}\equiv\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{\bar{M}_{P}^{2}},

(193)

with
M_{P}
the Planck mass. Such a ratio indicates that when the mass of spin gauge field is much smaller than the Planck mass, i.e.,
m_{G}^{2}\ll\bar{M}_{P}^{2}
and
\gamma_{G}\ll 1
, the resulting new effect only brings a tiny modification to the general relativity. It is expected that the future experiments could provide a reliable constraint on the mass of spin gauge field.

Let us now discuss an alternative case that the considering energy scale
\mu_{G}
is higher than the mass scale of spin gauge field, i.e.,
\mu_{G}>m_{A}
. For such a short distance case, it is useful to take into account the gravitational origin of spin gauge symmetry, which brings the spin gauge field decomposed into two parts as shown in Eq.(
58
), i.e.,
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}\equiv\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu}^{ab}+\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}
. By applying for such a decomposition of spin gauge field with the replacement
\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}\to g_{G}\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}
, we can rewrite the gravidynamic equation given in Eq.(
171
) into the following form:

\displaystyle R_{\mu\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\chi_{\mu\nu}R+8\pi G_{N}\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}=-8\pi G_{N}\mspace{1mu}(T_{\mu\nu}+\check{T}_{\mu\nu}),

(194)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}=(\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}})\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}R_{\rho\sigma\lambda^{\prime}}^{\lambda}R_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\lambda}^{\lambda^{\prime}}=R_{\mu\rho}^{\;\sigma\lambda}R_{\;\nu\sigma\lambda}^{\rho}-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}R_{\rho\sigma}^{\;\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}R_{\;\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}^{\sigma\rho},

\displaystyle\check{T}_{\mu\nu}=-(\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}})\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}(2g_{G}^{-1}\mathsf{F}_{\rho\sigma}^{ab}R_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}ab}+\mathsf{F}_{\rho\sigma}^{ab}\mathsf{F}_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}ab})+m_{G}^{2}g_{G}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\;\,(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}

\displaystyle\qquad+m_{G}^{2}g_{G}^{2}[\frac{1}{2}(\chi_{\mu\lambda}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho}+\chi_{\nu\lambda}\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho})\bar{\chi}^{[\lambda\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\hat{\chi}_{\mu\nu}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}]\mathsf{A}_{[\rho\sigma]}^{a}\mathsf{A}_{[\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]}^{a^{\prime}},

(195)

and

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\rho}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\;\,(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}\equiv\partial_{\rho}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}-\Gamma_{\rho\lambda}^{\rho}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\lambda}-\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\lambda\nu}^{\rho}-\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\lambda\mu}^{\rho}-\frac{1}{2}g_{G}(\mathsf{A}_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\mu\lambda}^{\rho}+\mathsf{A}_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\nu\lambda}^{\rho}),

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}\equiv\chi_{\mu\sigma}\chi_{\nu}^{\;\;a}\bar{\chi}^{[\rho\sigma]\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}\mathsf{A}_{[\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}]}^{a^{\prime}},\quad\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{(\mu\nu)}^{\rho}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}+\bar{\mathsf{A}}_{\nu\mu}^{\rho}),\quad\mathsf{A}_{[\rho\sigma]}^{a}\equiv\mathsf{A}_{\rho b}^{a}\chi_{\sigma}^{\;b}-\mathsf{A}_{\sigma b}^{a}\chi_{\rho}^{\;b},

(196)

with
\mathsf{F}_{\rho\sigma}^{ab}
and
R_{\rho\sigma}^{ab}
defined in Eq.(
V.1
).

It is seen that the tensor
\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}
is solely described by the Riemann tensor
R_{\mu\nu\sigma}^{\rho}
, while the tensor
\check{T}_{\mu\nu}
is proportionally given by the spin covariant gauge field
\mathsf{A}_{\mu}^{ab}
. Actually, the tensor
\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}
provides the radiative energy momentum of gravitational field with the following traceless property:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}=\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}(\eta_{\mu}^{\;\rho}\eta_{\nu}^{\;\rho^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{4}\chi_{\mu\nu}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\rho^{\prime}})\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\sigma^{\prime}}\mspace{1mu}R_{\rho\sigma\lambda^{\prime}}^{\lambda}R_{\rho^{\prime}\sigma^{\prime}\lambda}^{\lambda^{\prime}}=0.

(197)

As the tensor term
\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}
concerns the quadratic form of Riemann tensor, it involves high derivatives over the gravimetric field. Therefore, when the energy scale
\mu_{G}
is considered to be much below the Planck scale but above the mass scale
m_{A}
of spin gauge field, the effect of gravidynamics is small, i.e.:

\displaystyle 8\pi G_{N}\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}\ll R_{\mu\nu},\quad m_{A}<\mu_{G}\ll M_{P},

(198)

Nevertheless, once the energy scale
\mu_{G}
is compatible to the Planck mass scale, such an effect becomes significant:

\displaystyle 8\pi G_{N}\widetilde{G}_{\mu\nu}\sim R_{\mu\nu},\quad m_{A}<\mu_{G}\sim M_{P}.

(199)

In conclusion, at a very short distance close to or even smaller than the Planck length
l_{P}=1/M_{P}
, the new effect caused from the gravidynamics based on the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry will get significantly important, it may even change the statement on the singularity of spacetime based on the Einstein theory of general relativity. It is interesting to make a quantitive study and general proof, while it is beyond the scope of present paper and will be investigated elsewhere. Actually, our starting point in this paper is mainly based on the consideration of effective theory. When approaching to the Planck scale, we should begin with exploring a more fundamental theory which is expected to involve more contents of new physics.

IX
Spinodynamics via equations of motion of spin gauge field in biframe spacetime

Following along the least action principle and taking the spin gauge field and gravigauge field as independent degrees of freedom, we are able to derive the following equations of motion to describe the spinodynamics:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\boldsymbol{\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}-\frac{m_{G}^{2}}{M_{\kappa}^{2}}\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{a}^{\mu\nu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu b}=\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}_{ab}^{\mu},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}-m_{G}^{2}g_{G}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{a}^{\mu\nu}\chi_{\nu b}=\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{ab}^{\mu},

(200)

where the second equation is resulted by making the scaling gauge fixing condition to be in Einstein basis. The field strength and gauge covariant derivative are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}g_{G}^{-2}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}ab},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\boldsymbol{\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\partial_{\nu}\boldsymbol{\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}-{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu a}^{c}\boldsymbol{\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}}_{cb}^{\mu\nu},

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathcal{F}}}_{a}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

(201)

and

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\chi\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}ab},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{\nu}\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\partial_{\nu}\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{ab}^{\mu\nu}-g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{\nu a}^{c}\hat{{\mathcal{F}}}_{cb}^{\mu\nu},

\displaystyle\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{a}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\chi\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

(202)

with

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\bar{\mathsf{H}}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\nu\mu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}-\bar{\chi}_{aa^{\prime}}^{\nu\mu\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}).

(203)

Where
\bar{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}
and
\bar{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}_{aa^{\prime}}
are defined in Eqs.(
VI.3
) and (
VI.3
), respectively. The field strengths
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{ab}
,
{\mathcal{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}
and
{\mathbf{F}}_{\mu\nu}^{a}
are defined correspondingly in Eqs.(
III.1
), (
III.1
) and (
V.2
). The corresponding currents are given by the following explicit forms:

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}_{ab}^{\mu}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathbf{A}}^{c\mu}\bar{\Psi}_{-}\Sigma_{cab}\Psi_{-},

\displaystyle\hat{{\mathsf{J}}}_{ab}^{\mu}\equiv\chi\hat{\chi}^{c\mu}g_{G}\bar{\psi}\Sigma_{cab}\psi.

(204)

The above equations of motion characterize the spinodynamics of spin gauge field.

The inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry brings the spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}
as a vector field in Minkowski spacetime to couple with Dirac fermion via a vector gauge field
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{A}}}_{c}^{ab}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{c}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}
or
{\mathcal{A}}_{c}^{ab}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\mu}{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu}^{ab}
due to the presence of gravigauge field, which is regarded as a vector spin gauge field defined in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, which motivates us to derive the equation of motion of spin gauge field in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. The equation of motion for such a spin gauge field can directly be derived from the actions presented in Eqs.(
85
) and (
88
) based on the least action principle. In the Einstein basis, the equation of motion is obtained as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{d}{\mathcal{F}}_{ab}^{\;cd}-m_{G}^{2}g_{G}\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{a}^{cd}\eta_{db}={\mathsf{J}}_{ab}^{\;c},

(205)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{ab}^{cd}\equiv\eta^{cc^{\prime}}\eta^{dd^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}ab},\quad\bar{{\mathbf{F}}}_{a}^{cd}\equiv\bar{\eta}_{aa^{\prime}}^{[cd]c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}{\mathbf{F}}_{c^{\prime}d^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{d}{\mathcal{F}}_{ab}^{\;cd}\equiv\eth_{d}{\mathcal{F}}_{ab}^{\;cd}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{dc^{\prime}}^{c}{\mathcal{F}}_{ab}^{\;c^{\prime}d}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{dd^{\prime}}^{d}{\mathcal{F}}_{ab}^{\;cd^{\prime}}-g_{G}{\mathcal{A}}_{da}^{a^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{a^{\prime}b}^{\;cd},

(206)

for the field strength and gauge covariant derivative, and

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}_{ab}^{\;c}\equiv g_{G}\eta^{cd}\bar{\psi}\Sigma_{dab}\psi,

(207)

for the corresponding current.

By applying the relations shown in Eqs.(
183
)-(
VIII.5
), we are able to rewrite the above equation of motion into the following forms:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{d}{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;[cab]}^{d}+m_{T}^{2}{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}=m_{T}^{2}g_{G}^{-1}\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cab]}-{\mathsf{J}}_{cab},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{D}}_{d}{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;(cab]}^{d}+m_{S}^{2}{\mathcal{A}}_{(cab]}=m_{S}^{2}g_{G}^{-1}\mathsf{\Omega}_{(cab]},

(208)

with

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;cab}^{d}\equiv\eta^{dd^{\prime}}{\mathcal{F}}_{d^{\prime}cab}\equiv{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;[cab]}^{d}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;(cab]}^{d},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;[cab]}^{d}\equiv\frac{1}{3}({\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;cab}^{d}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;abc}^{d}+{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;bca}^{d}),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;(cab]}^{d}\equiv\frac{1}{3}(2{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;cab}^{d}-{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;abc}^{d}-{\mathcal{F}}_{\;\;bca}^{d}),

\displaystyle{\mathsf{J}}_{cab}\equiv g_{G}\bar{\psi}\Sigma_{cab}\psi,

(209)

where the totally antisymmetric spin gauge field
{\mathcal{A}}_{[cab]}
with mass
m_{T}
reflects the so-called torsion like field.

The above equations of motion for the spin gauge field describe the spinodynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, which can also be derived from Eq.(
IX
) by projecting it into locally flat gravigauge spacetime via the gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}
.

X
Electrodynamics in gravitational quantum field theory and generalized Maxwell equations in biframe spacetime

Based on the maximal joint symmetry for the action of chirality-based Dirac spinor, namely the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry and inhomogeneous Lorentz symmetry (or Poincaré symmetry), we have provided a detailed analysis and investigation on the gravitational relativistic quantum theory of Dirac spinor as well as the gravidynamics and spinodynamics, which brings about the theoretical framework of gravitational quantum field theory. In this section, we are going to investigate systematically the electrodynamics in the presence of gravitational interaction and to derive the generalized Maxwell equations in Minkowski spacetime of coordinates and also in locally flat gravigauge spacetime spanned by the gravigauge basis.

X.1
Gravigeometry-medium electrodynamics and generalized Maxwell equations with gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic field

Following along the least action principle, we obtain the following equations of motion for the electromagnetic gauge field:

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\widehat{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\mu\nu}=-\widehat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\mu},

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\widehat{F}^{\mu\nu}=-\widehat{J}^{\mu},

(210)

with the field strengths and currents defined by

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\widehat{F}^{\mu\nu}\equiv\chi\hat{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}F_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}-\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\nu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\nu^{\prime}}),

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\chi}^{\mu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\nu^{\prime}}-\hat{\chi}^{\nu\mu^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu^{\prime}}),

(211)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\mu}\equiv\mathfrak{A}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}{\mathbf{J}}^{a},\quad{\mathbf{J}}^{a}=-g_{E}\bar{\Psi}_{-}\Gamma^{a}\Psi_{-},

\displaystyle\widehat{J}^{\mu}\equiv\chi\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}J^{a},\quad J^{a}=-g_{E}\bar{\psi}\gamma^{a}\psi.

(212)

The second equation in Eq.(
171
) holds in the Einstein basis for the gauge fixing condition of scaling gauge symmetry. Where
\widehat{{\mathbf{F}}}^{\mu\nu}
(
\widehat{F}^{\mu\nu}
) and
\widehat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\mu}
(
\widehat{J}^{\mu}
) define the electromagnetic gauge field strength and the corresponding current density in the presence of gravimetric field, and
{\mathbf{J}}^{a}
(
J^{a}
) is regarded as a free current density in the absence of gravitational interaction.

Meanwhile, the gauge field strength
F_{\mu\nu}
satisfies the Bianchi identity,

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\nu}F_{\rho\sigma}=0.

(213)

The above equations of motion in the Einstein basis can be rewritten into the following four sets of equations:

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{E}}=\widehat{J}^{0},

\displaystyle\nabla\times\widehat{\mathbf{B}}=\boldsymbol{\widehat{J}}+\frac{\partial\widehat{\mathbf{E}}}{\partial x^{0}},

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{B}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{E}=-\frac{\partial\mathbf{B}}{\partial x^{0}},

(214)

where we have introduced the definitions:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{E}}\equiv(\widehat{E}^{1},\widehat{E}^{2},\widehat{E}^{3})=(-\widehat{F}^{01},-\widehat{F}^{02},-\widehat{F}^{03}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\equiv(\widehat{B}^{1},\widehat{B}^{2},\widehat{B}^{3})=(-\widehat{F}^{23},-\widehat{F}^{31},-\widehat{F}^{12}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{E}\equiv(E^{1},E^{2},E^{3})=(-F^{01},-F^{02},-F^{03}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}\equiv(B^{1},B^{2},B^{3})=(-F^{23},-F^{31},-F^{12}).

(215)

The vector fields
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
define the electric field and magnetic field in the presence of gravimetric field which is introduced to characterize the Riemann geometry of curved spacetime. For convenience, we may refer to
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
as
gravigeometry-medium electric field
and
gravigeometry-medium magnetic field
, respectively. The generalized Maxwell equations presented in Eq.(
X.1
) may be mentioned as
gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations
, and the corresponding electrodynamics is referred to as
gravigeometry-medium electrodynamics
.

In the ordinary Maxwell equations yielded in globally flat Minkowski spacetime, the corresponding field strength with
\mathbf{E}\equiv\{E^{i}=-F^{0i}\}
(i=1,2,3) and
\mathbf{B}\equiv\{B^{i}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{i}_{\;\;jk}F^{jk}\}
(i,j,k=1,2,3) defines a free-motion electromagnetic field in the absence of gravitational interaction. In the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations shown in Eq.(
X.1
) due to the presence of gravimetric field, the field strength defined via
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}\equiv\{\widehat{E}^{i}=-\widehat{F}^{0i}\}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\equiv\{\widehat{B}^{i}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{i}_{\;\;jk}\widehat{F}^{jk}\}
are regarded as gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic field. The relations between the gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic field and the free motion electromagnetic field can directly be written down from the definitions in Eq.(
III.1
) as follows:

\displaystyle\widehat{F}^{0i}=\chi(\hat{\chi}^{00}\hat{\chi}^{ij}-\hat{\chi}^{0i}\hat{\chi}^{0j})F_{0j}+\chi\hat{\chi}^{ik}\hat{\chi}^{0j}F_{jk}\equiv-\widehat{E}^{i},

\displaystyle\widehat{F}^{ij}=\chi(\hat{\chi}^{i0}\hat{\chi}^{jk}-\hat{\chi}^{j0}\hat{\chi}^{ik})F_{0k}+\chi\hat{\chi}^{ik}\hat{\chi}^{jl}F_{kl}\equiv\epsilon^{ij}_{\;\;\;k}\widehat{B}^{k},

(216)

which can be expressed into the following simple forms:

\displaystyle\widehat{E}^{i}=\hat{\varepsilon}^{i}_{\;j}E^{j}+\hat{\alpha}^{i}_{\;j}B^{j},

\displaystyle\widehat{B}^{i}=\hat{\mu}^{i}_{\;j}B^{j}-\hat{\alpha}^{\;\,i}_{j}E^{j},

(217)

with the coefficient functions:

\displaystyle\hat{\varepsilon}^{i}_{\;j}(x)\equiv\chi(\hat{\chi}^{0}_{\;0}\hat{\chi}^{i}_{\;j}-\hat{\chi}^{i}_{\;0}\hat{\chi}^{0}_{\;j}),

\displaystyle\hat{\mu}^{i}_{\;j}(x)\equiv-\frac{1}{2}\chi\epsilon^{i}_{\;\;kl}\hat{\chi}^{kk^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{ll^{\prime}}\epsilon_{k^{\prime}l^{\prime}j},

\displaystyle\hat{\alpha}^{i}_{\;j}(x)\equiv\chi\hat{\chi}^{ik}\hat{\chi}^{l0}\epsilon_{klj}.

(218)

Note that all the coefficients are functions of coordinate spacetime and they are in general the vectors and tensors in spatial dimensions.

It is useful to rewrite the above relations into the following forms in the vector representation:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{E}}\equiv\widehat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\cdot\mathbf{E}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\cdot\mathbf{B},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\equiv\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}\cdot\mathbf{B}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{T}\cdot\mathbf{E},

(219)

with the bi-vector coefficient functions:

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\varepsilon}^{i}_{\;j}(x)\},\quad\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\mu}^{i}_{\;j}(x)\},

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\alpha}^{i}_{\;j}(x)\},\quad\widehat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{T}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\alpha}_{j}^{\;i}(x)\}.

(220)

These coefficient functions are in principle governed by the gravidynamics.

X.2
Gravigauge-mediated electrodynamics and generalized Maxwell equations in locally flat gravigauge spacetime

In the absence of gravitational interaction, the electromagnetic gauge field
A_{\mu}
is regarded as a vector field in globally flat Minkowski spacetime. The inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry with the introduction of gravigauge field
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}
) brings
A_{\mu}
to couple with Dirac fermion via a vector gauge field
A_{a}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}A_{\mu}
defined in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. The least action principle leads the equation of motion of gauge field
A_{a}
to have the following form in locally flat gravigauge spacetime:

\displaystyle D_{b}F^{ab}=-J^{a},

(221)

with the field strength and covariant derivative defined as follows:

\displaystyle D_{b}F^{ab}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\eth_{b}F^{ab}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{bc}^{a}F^{cb}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{bc}^{b}F^{ac},

\displaystyle F^{ab}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\eta^{ac}\eta^{bd}F_{cd}\equiv\hat{\chi}^{a\mu}\hat{\chi}^{b\nu}F_{\mu\nu},\quad F_{\mu\nu}=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu},

\displaystyle F_{cd}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle D_{c}A_{d}-D_{d}A_{c}=\eth_{c}A_{d}-\eth_{d}A_{c}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{cd}^{b}A_{b}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{dc}^{b}A_{b},

(222)

and the current density is simply given by

\displaystyle J^{a}=-g_{E}\bar{\psi}\gamma^{a}\psi,

(223)

which is considered as the current density for freely moving Dirac fermion in locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

The field strength
F_{ab}
satisfies the following identity:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{cabd}D_{c}F_{ab}=0,

(224)

which results from the Bianchi identity of Riemann curvature tensor
\epsilon^{cabd}R_{cab}^{b^{\prime}}=0
in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. It can directly be verified as follows:

\displaystyle\epsilon^{cabd}D_{c}F_{ab}=2\epsilon^{cabd}D_{c}(D_{a}A_{b})=-\epsilon^{cabd}R_{cab}^{b^{\prime}}A_{b^{\prime}}=0.

(225)

It can be proved from Eq.(
221
) that the current density
J^{a}
is conserved in a gauge covariant form, i.e.:

\displaystyle D_{a}J^{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-D_{a}D_{b}F^{ab}=\frac{1}{2}(R_{abc}^{a}F^{cb}+R_{abc}^{b}F^{ac})

(226)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{cb}F^{cb}=\frac{1}{2}(R_{cb}-R_{bc})F^{cb}=0,

where the last equality is due to the symmetric feature of Ricci curvature tensor
R_{cb}=R_{bc}
and the antisymmetric property of field strength
F^{cb}=-F^{bc}
. It is straightforward to write down the quadratic form for the equation of motion of gauge field
A_{a}
shown in Eq.(
221
) as follows:

\displaystyle D_{c}D^{c}A_{a}+R_{ac}A^{c}-D_{a}(D_{c}A^{c})=J_{a}.

(227)

When taking the following covariant gauge fixing condition:

\displaystyle D_{c}A^{c}=\eth_{c}A^{c}+\mathsf{\Omega}_{cd}^{c}A^{d}=0,

(228)

and utilizing the equation of motion of gravidynamics presented in Eq.(
176
), we arrive at the following equation of motion for the gauge field
A_{a}
in locally flat gravigauge spacetime:

\displaystyle D_{c}D^{c}A_{a}+\frac{1}{2}R\mspace{1mu}A_{a}-8\pi G_{N}(T_{ac}+\widetilde{T}_{ac})A^{c}=J_{a},

(229)

which indicates that a non-zero Ricci curvature scalar
R\neq 0
brings on an effective mass to the electromagnetic gauge field
A_{a}
when it propagates in locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

It is useful to rewrite the equations of motion in Eqs.(
221
) and (
224
) into the following forms:

\displaystyle\eth_{b}F^{ba}-\breve{A}_{b}F^{ba}-\frac{1}{2}F_{bc}^{a}F^{bc}=J^{a},

\displaystyle\epsilon^{abcd}(\eth_{b}F_{cd}+F_{bc}^{c^{\prime}}F_{c^{\prime}d})=0,

(230)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle F_{bc}^{a}\equiv-\mathsf{\Omega}_{[bc]}^{a}=\mathsf{\Omega}_{cb}^{a}-\mathsf{\Omega}_{bc}^{a}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\mu}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\nu}F_{\mu\nu}^{a}\equiv\hat{F}_{cb}^{\mu}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a},

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{cb}^{\mu}\equiv\eth_{c}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\mu}-\eth_{b}\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu},\quad\eth_{b}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}\partial_{\nu},

\displaystyle\breve{A}_{b}\equiv F_{cb}^{c}\equiv-\mathsf{\Omega}_{cb}^{c}=-\hat{\chi}\partial_{\nu}(\chi\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}),

(231)

where
F_{bc}^{a}
is the gravigauge field strength in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. The equations of motion in locally flat gravigauge spacetime with
a,b=(0,\alpha)
(
\alpha=1,2,3
) can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\eth_{\alpha}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}-\breve{A}_{\alpha}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}-\breve{E}_{\alpha}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}=J^{0}-\breve{B}_{\alpha}\widetilde{B}^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\beta\alpha\gamma}(\eth_{\beta}\widetilde{B}_{\gamma}-\breve{A}_{\beta}\widetilde{B}_{\gamma})+\breve{B}_{\beta}^{\;\;\alpha}\widetilde{B}^{\beta}=J^{\alpha}+\eth_{0}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}-\breve{A}_{0}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}+\breve{E}_{\beta}^{\;\;\alpha}\widetilde{E}^{\beta},

\displaystyle\eth_{\alpha}\widetilde{B}^{\alpha}+2\breve{A}_{\alpha}\widetilde{B}^{\alpha}+2\breve{E}_{\alpha}\widetilde{B}^{\alpha}=\breve{B}_{\alpha}\widetilde{E}^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\eth_{0}\widetilde{B}^{\alpha}+2\breve{A}_{0}\widetilde{B}^{\alpha}-2\breve{E}_{\beta}^{\;\;\alpha}\widetilde{B}^{\beta}=-\epsilon^{\beta\alpha\gamma}(\eth_{\beta}\widetilde{E}_{\gamma}+\breve{E}_{\beta}\widetilde{E}_{\gamma})-\breve{B}^{\;\;\alpha}_{\beta}\widetilde{E}^{\beta},

(232)

with the field strengths defined to be:

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}=-F^{0\alpha},\quad\widetilde{B}^{\alpha}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\alpha}_{\;\;\beta\gamma}F^{\beta\gamma},

\displaystyle\breve{E}_{\alpha}=-F_{0\alpha}^{0},\quad\breve{B}_{\alpha}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\alpha}^{\;\;\gamma\delta}F_{\gamma\delta}^{0},

\displaystyle\breve{E}_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}=-F_{0\alpha}^{\beta},\quad\breve{B}_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\alpha}^{\;\;\gamma\delta}F_{\gamma\delta}^{\beta}.

(233)

The field strengths
\widetilde{E}^{\alpha}
and
\widetilde{B}^{\alpha}
are defined as electromagnetic field in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, which may be called as
gravigauge-dressed electric field
and
gravigauge-dressed magnetic field
, respectively, in order to distinguish with the free-motion electromagnetic field
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
defined in Minkowski spacetime.
\breve{E}_{\alpha}
and
\breve{B}_{\alpha}
as well as
\breve{E}_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}
and
\breve{B}_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}
are defined as
electromagnetic-like gravigauge fields
to characterize the gravitational interactions. For convenience,
\breve{E}_{\alpha}
and
\breve{B}_{\alpha}
are referred to as
electric-like gravigauge field
and
magnetic-like gravigauge field
, respectively, while
\breve{E}_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}
and
\breve{B}_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}
are mentioned as
electric-like gravigauge tensor field
and
magnetic-like gravigauge tensor field
, respectively.
\breve{A}_{0}
and
\breve{A}_{\alpha}
are related to the electromagnetic-like gravigauge field as follows:

\displaystyle\breve{A}_{0}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}\breve{E}_{\beta}^{\;\;\alpha},\quad\breve{A}_{\alpha}=-\breve{E}_{\alpha}-\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\;\;\,\gamma}\breve{B}_{\gamma}^{\;\;\beta}.

(234)

The equations of motion given in Eqs.(
221
) and (
224
) or Eqs.(
X.2
)-(
X.3
) characterize the electrodynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, which may be referred to as
gravigauge-mediated electrodynamics
.

In light of vector representation, the equations of motion for the gravigauge-dressed electromagnetic fields
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}
as shown in Eq.(
X.2
) can be rewritten into the following forms in locally flat gravigauge spacetime:

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{\mathbf{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}=J^{0}+\breve{\mathbf{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+\breve{\mathbb{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{J}}+\eth_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{A}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}+\breve{\mathbf{E}}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{\mathbb{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}=-\eth_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}-2\breve{A}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+2\breve{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+2\breve{\mathbf{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}=\breve{\mathbf{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}},

(235)

where we have introduced the following vector-like notations:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}\equiv(\widetilde{E}^{1},\widetilde{E}^{2},\widetilde{E}^{3}),\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}\equiv(\widetilde{B}^{1},\widetilde{B}^{2},\widetilde{B}^{3}),

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{E}}\equiv(\breve{E}_{1},\breve{E}_{2},\breve{E}_{3}),\quad\breve{\mathbf{B}}\equiv(\breve{B}_{1},\breve{B}_{2},\breve{B}_{3}),

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\alpha}\equiv(\breve{E}_{1}^{\;\;\alpha},\breve{E}_{2}^{\;\;\alpha},\breve{E}_{3}^{\;\;\alpha}),\quad\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha}\equiv(\breve{B}_{1}^{\;\;\alpha},\breve{B}_{2}^{\;\;\alpha},\breve{B}_{3}^{\;\;\alpha}),

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{A}}\equiv(\breve{A}_{1},\breve{A}_{2},\breve{A}_{3}),

(236)

and

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbb{E}}\equiv\{\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\alpha}\}\equiv\{\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{1},\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{2},\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{3}\}

\displaystyle\quad\equiv\{(\breve{E}_{1}^{\;\;1},\breve{E}_{2}^{\;\;1},\breve{E}_{3}^{\;\;1}),(\breve{E}_{1}^{\;\;2},\breve{E}_{2}^{\;\;2},\breve{E}_{3}^{\;\;2}),(\breve{E}_{1}^{\;\;3},\breve{E}_{2}^{\;\;3},\breve{E}_{3}^{\;\;3})\},

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbb{B}}\equiv\{\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\alpha}\}\equiv\{\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{1},\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{2},\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{3}\}

\displaystyle\quad\equiv\{(\breve{B}_{1}^{\;\;1},\breve{B}_{2}^{\;\;1},\breve{B}_{3}^{\;\;1}),(\breve{B}_{1}^{\;\;2},\breve{B}_{2}^{\;\;2},\breve{B}_{3}^{\;\;2}),(\breve{B}_{1}^{\;\;3},\breve{B}_{2}^{\;\;3},\breve{B}_{3}^{\;\;3})\},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\equiv(\eth_{1},\eth_{2},\eth_{3})\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;0}\partial_{0}+\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;i}\partial_{i}\},\quad\eth_{0}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;\mu}\partial_{\mu}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;0}\partial_{0}+\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;i}\partial_{i},

(237)

with
\breve{\mathbb{E}}
and
\breve{\mathbb{B}}
denoting electromagnetic-like gravigauge bi-vector fields. Where
\breve{\nabla}
and
\eth_{0}
represent the gravi-coordinate derivatives, which are related to the ordinary coordinate derivatives as follows:

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\equiv(\eth_{1},\eth_{2},\eth_{3})\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;0}\partial_{0}+\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;i}\partial_{i}\}\equiv\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}+\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla,

\displaystyle\eth_{0}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;\mu}\partial_{\mu}=\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;0}\partial_{0}+\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;i}\partial_{i}\equiv\breve{\kappa}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla,

(238)

with the definitions:

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;0}\}\equiv(\hat{\chi}_{1}^{\;\;0},\hat{\chi}_{2}^{\;\;0},\hat{\chi}_{3}^{\;\;0}),\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{\alpha}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;i}\}\equiv(\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;1},\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;2},\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;3}),

\displaystyle\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{1},\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{2},\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{3}\}\equiv\{(\hat{\chi}_{1}^{\;\;1},\hat{\chi}_{1}^{\;\;2},\hat{\chi}_{1}^{\;\;3}),(\hat{\chi}_{2}^{\;\;1},\hat{\chi}_{2}^{\;\;2},\hat{\chi}_{2}^{\;\;3}),(\hat{\chi}_{3}^{\;\;1},\hat{\chi}_{3}^{\;\;2},\hat{\chi}_{3}^{\;\;3})\},

\displaystyle\breve{\kappa}(x)\equiv\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;0},\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}(x)\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;i}\}\equiv(\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;1},\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;2},\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;3}).

(239)

In terms of the above definitions and notations for the gravi-coordinate derivatives, the gravigauge Maxwell equations in Eq.(
X.2
) can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}}{\partial x^{0}}+(\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla)\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{\mathbf{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}=J^{0}+\breve{\mathbf{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\times\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}}{\partial x^{0}}+(\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla)\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+\breve{\mathbb{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{J}}+\breve{\kappa}\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}}{\partial x^{0}}+(\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{A}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}},

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\times\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}}{\partial x^{0}}+(\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla)\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}+\breve{\mathbf{E}}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-\breve{\mathbb{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}=-\breve{\kappa}\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}}{\partial x^{0}}-(\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}-\breve{A}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-2\breve{A}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\frac{\partial\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}}{\partial x^{0}}+(\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla)\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+2\breve{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}+2\breve{\mathbf{E}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}=\breve{\mathbf{B}}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}.

(240)

It is noticed that the equations in Eq.(
X.2
) and Eq.(
X.2
) (or Eq.(
X.2
)) bring on the generalized Maxwell equations in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. To distinguish with the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations presented in Eq.(
X.3
) and Eq.(
X.1
)-(
X.1
) in coordinate spacetime, they may be referred to as
gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations
which are derived to describe the
gravigauge-mediated electrodynamics
in locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

The gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations show explicitly how the gravigauge-dressed electromagnetic fields
\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}
interact directly with the electromagnetic-like gravigauge fields
\breve{\mathbf{E}}
and
\breve{\mathbf{B}}
as well as electromagnetic-like gravigauge vector fields
\breve{\mathbb{E}}
and
\breve{\mathbb{B}}
in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, which reveals how the gravitational effect emerges to bring the gravigauge-mediated electrodynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

X.3
Gravimetric-gauge-mediated electrodynamics and generalized Maxwell equations in dynamic Riemannian spacetime

The equations of motion for the gauge field
A_{\mu}
can also be expressed as the following form in dynamic Riemannian spacetime:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}=-J_{\mu},

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}F_{\mu\nu}=0,

(241)

with the covariant derivative defined as follows:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}\equiv\hat{\chi}^{\nu\rho}\nabla_{\rho}F_{\mu\nu}=\hat{\chi}^{\nu\rho}(\partial_{\rho}F_{\mu\nu}-\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}F_{\lambda\nu}-\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda}).

(242)

The second equation in Eq.(
X.3
) is the Bianchi identity which can also be rewritten in terms of the covariant derivative:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\partial_{\rho}F_{\mu\nu}\equiv\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\nabla_{\rho}F_{\mu\nu}\equiv\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}(\partial_{\rho}F_{\mu\nu}-\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}F_{\lambda\nu}-\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\lambda}F_{\mu\lambda})=0,

(243)

where we have used the symmetric property of the affine connection (Christoffel symbol)
\Gamma_{\rho\mu}^{\lambda}=\Gamma_{\mu\rho}^{\lambda}
.

The current density in Eq.(
X.3
) is defined by,

\displaystyle J_{\mu}\equiv\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}J_{a}=-g_{E}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}\bar{\psi}\gamma_{a}\psi.

(244)

which is conserved via the covariant derivative:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}J_{\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\nabla^{\mu}\nabla^{\nu}F_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}(R^{\mu\nu\rho}_{\mu}F_{\rho\nu}+R^{\mu\nu\rho}_{\nu}F_{\mu\rho})=R^{\mu\rho}F_{\mu\rho}=0,

(245)

where the last equality is due to the symmetric feature of Ricci curvature tensor
R^{\mu\rho}=R^{\rho\mu}
and the antisymmetric property of electromagnetic field strength
F_{\mu\rho}=-F_{\rho\mu}
.

In can be checked that the equation of motion of gauge field
A_{\mu}
gets the following form:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}A_{\mu}+R_{\mu\nu}A^{\nu}-\nabla_{\mu}(\nabla^{\nu}A_{\nu})=J_{\mu}.

(246)

By imposing the covariant gauge fixing condition
\nabla^{\nu}A_{\nu}=0
, we come to the following equation of motion for the gauge field
A_{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}A_{\mu}+R_{\mu\nu}A^{\nu}=J_{\mu}.

(247)

When applying for the geometric gravitational equation shown in Eq.(
171
), we have,

\displaystyle(\nabla^{\nu}\nabla_{\nu}+\frac{1}{2}R)A_{\mu}-8\pi G_{N}(T_{\mu\nu}+\widetilde{T}_{\mu\nu})A^{\nu}=J_{\mu},

(248)

which indicates that a non-zero Ricci curvature scalar
R\neq 0
leads to an effective mass-like term for the electromagnetic gauge field
A_{\mu}
when it is propagating in a curved Riemannian spacetime.

To reflect the effect of geometric gravidynamics on the electrodynamics, it is useful to express the equations of motion in Eq.(
X.3
) into the following form:

\displaystyle(\hat{\partial}^{\nu}+\check{A}^{\nu})F_{\nu\mu}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\nu}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma\nu^{\prime}}F_{\rho\sigma}^{\lambda}\eta_{\lambda\mu}F_{\nu\nu^{\prime}}=J_{\mu},

(249)

where we have introduced the definitions:

\displaystyle F_{\rho\sigma}^{\lambda}\equiv\partial_{\rho}\chi_{\sigma}^{\;\lambda}-\partial_{\sigma}\chi_{\rho}^{\;\lambda},\quad\chi_{\rho}^{\;\lambda}\equiv\chi_{\rho\mu}\eta^{\mu\lambda},

\displaystyle\check{A}^{\nu}\equiv\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{\nu\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\sigma}F_{\rho\sigma}^{\lambda}\eta_{\mu\lambda}\equiv\hat{\partial}^{\nu}\ln\chi+\partial_{\rho}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\nu},

\displaystyle\hat{\partial}^{\nu}\equiv\hat{\chi}^{\nu\rho}\partial_{\rho}.

(250)

Such a defined
F_{\rho\sigma}^{\lambda}
is considered to be a gauge field strength when viewing the gravimetric field
\chi_{\rho}^{\;\lambda}
to be a gauge-type field (which is actually a combined field of gauvigauge field
\chi_{\rho}^{\;\lambda}\equiv\chi_{\rho}^{\;a}\chi_{\;a}^{\lambda}
). For convenience of mention,
\chi_{\rho}^{\;\lambda}
is referred to as
gravimetric-gauge field
and
F_{\rho\sigma}^{\lambda}
is called as
electromagnetic-like gravimetric-gauge field strength
.

The equations of electromagnetic field in dynamic Riemannian spacetime are obtained as follows:

\displaystyle(\hat{\partial}^{i}+\check{A}^{i})E_{i}+\hat{\chi}^{\rho i}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma 0}F_{\rho\sigma}^{0}E_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{\rho j}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma k}F_{\rho\sigma}^{0}\epsilon_{jk}^{\;\;\;\;i}B_{i}=J_{0},

\displaystyle(\hat{\partial}^{j}+\check{A}^{j})\epsilon_{jk}^{\;\;\;\;\;i}B_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{\chi}^{\rho k}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma l}F_{\rho\sigma i}\epsilon_{kl}^{\;\;\;\;j}B_{j}=J_{i}+(\hat{\partial}^{0}+\check{A}^{0})E_{i}+\hat{\chi}^{\rho 0}\hat{\chi}^{\sigma j}F_{\rho\sigma i}E_{j},

(251)

which can simply be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle(\hat{\partial}^{i}+\check{A}^{i})E_{i}+\check{{\mathcal{E}}}^{i}E_{i}+\check{\mathcal{B}}^{i}B_{i}=J_{0},

\displaystyle(\hat{\partial}^{j}+\check{A}^{j})\epsilon_{jk}^{\;\;\;\;\;i}B_{i}+B_{j}\check{\mathcal{B}}^{j}_{\;\;i}=J_{i}+(\hat{\partial}^{0}+\check{A}^{0})E_{i}-E_{j}\check{{\mathcal{E}}}^{j}_{\;\;i}.

(252)

Where we have introduced the definitions:

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{E}}}^{i}\equiv\varepsilon^{ij}\check{E}_{j}+\alpha^{ij}\check{B}_{j},

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{B}}^{i}\equiv\mu^{ij}\check{B}_{j}-\alpha^{ji}\check{E}_{j},

\displaystyle\check{{\mathcal{E}}}^{j}_{\;\;i}\equiv\varepsilon^{jk}\check{E}_{k\,i}+\alpha^{jk}\check{B}_{k\,i},

\displaystyle\check{\mathcal{B}}^{j}_{\;\;i}\equiv\mu^{jk}\check{B}_{k\,i}+\alpha^{kj}\check{E}_{k\,i},

(253)

with the coefficient functions and field strengths defined as follows:

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{ij}(x)\equiv\hat{\chi}^{ij}\hat{\chi}^{00}-\hat{\chi}^{i0}\hat{\chi}^{j0},

\displaystyle\mu^{ij}(x)\equiv\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{i}_{\;\;kl}\hat{\chi}^{kk^{\prime}}\hat{\chi}^{ll^{\prime}}\epsilon_{k^{\prime}l^{\prime}}^{\;\;\;\;\;j},

\displaystyle\alpha^{ij}(x)\equiv\hat{\chi}^{ik}\hat{\chi}^{0l}\epsilon^{j}_{\;\;kl},

(254)

and

\displaystyle\check{E}_{i}=-F_{0i}^{0},\quad\check{B}_{i}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{i}^{\;\;jl}F_{jl}^{0},

\displaystyle\check{E}_{k}^{\;\;i}=-F_{0k}^{i},\quad\check{B}_{k}^{\;\;i}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{k}^{\;\;jl}F_{jl}^{i}.

(255)

It is noticed that
\check{E}_{i}
and
\check{B}_{i}
as well as
\check{E}_{k}^{\;\;i}
and
\check{B}_{k}^{\;\;i}
are introduced as
electromagnetic-like gravimetric-gauge fields
to characterize the gravitational interactions in dynamic Riemannian spacetime. In analogous to electromagnetic field,
\check{E}_{i}
and
\check{B}_{i}
are referred to as
electric-like gravimetric-gauge field
and
magnetic-like gravimetric-gauge field
, respectively, while
\check{E}_{k}^{\;\;i}
and
\check{B}_{k}^{\;\;i}
are called as
electric-like gravimetric-gauge tensor field
and
magnetic-like gravimetric-gauge tensor field
, respectively.

In terms of vector representation, the equations presented in Eq.(
X.3
) and Eq.(
X.3
) can be rewritten into the following forms:

\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}\cdot\mathbf{E}+\check{\mathbf{A}}\cdot\mathbf{E}+\check{\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{E}}}}\cdot\mathbf{E}+\check{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}}\cdot\mathbf{B}=J_{0},

\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}\times\mathbf{B}+\check{\mathbf{A}}\times\mathbf{B}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}}\cdot\mathbf{B}={\mathbf{J}}+(\hat{\partial}^{0}+\check{A}^{0})\mathbf{E}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{E}}}}\cdot\mathbf{E},

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{B}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{E}=-\frac{\partial\mathbf{B}}{\partial x^{0}},

(256)

which bring on the generalized Maxwell equations in dynamic Riemannian spacetime. Where we have used the vector notations
\check{\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{E}}}}\equiv\{\check{{\mathcal{E}}}_{i}\}
,
\check{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}}\equiv\{\check{\mathcal{B}}_{i}\}
,
\check{\mathbf{A}}\equiv\{\check{\mathbf{A}}_{i}\}
,
\hat{\nabla}\equiv\{\hat{\partial}_{i}\}
and bi-vector notations
\widehat{\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{E}}}}\equiv\{\check{{\mathcal{E}}}_{j}^{\;\;i}\}
,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{B}}}\equiv\{\check{\mathcal{B}}_{j}^{\;\;i}\}
.

To distinguish with the gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations in locally flat gravigauge spacetime and gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations in coordinate spacetime, we may refer to the above generalized Maxwell equations as
gravimetric-gauge-mediated Maxwell equations
, which are provided to describe the
gravimetric-gauge-mediated electrodynamics
in dynamic Riemannian spacetime.

XI
General covariance of dynamic equations and electrodynamics in any motional and spinning reference frames

It is shown that the hermitian action constructed in locally flat gravigauge spacetime based on the gauge invariance principle becomes independent of the choice of coordinates. Namely, such an action holds in any coordinate system and gets invariant under the general coordinate transformation. In other word, such a gauge invariant action possesses automatically a hidden general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R) which is known to lay the foundation of Einstein’s general theory of relativity. Such a consequence indicates that the gauge invariance principle brings naturally on the emergence of general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R) as a local symmetry in coordinate spacetime.

XI.1
General covariance of dynamic equations

To be concrete, the general coordinate transformation is defined as an arbitrary reparametrization of coordinate systems, i.e.:

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}\equiv x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}(x),

(257)

which provides a local transformation in curved Riemannian spacetime and leads the displacement and derivative of coordinate systems to satisfy the following transformation laws:

\displaystyle dx^{\mu}\to dx^{{}^{\prime}\mu}=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\;\nu}\,dx^{\nu},\quad T^{\mu}_{\;\;\;\nu}\equiv\frac{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}}{\partial x^{\nu}},

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\to\partial^{\prime}_{\mu}\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}}=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}\,\partial_{\nu},\quad T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}\equiv\frac{\partial x^{\nu}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}},

(258)

which preserves the inner product to be invariant under the general coordinate transformation,

\displaystyle\langle\partial^{\prime}_{\mu},dx^{{}^{\prime}\nu}\rangle=T_{\mu}^{\;\,\rho}T^{\nu}_{\;\;\;\sigma}\langle\partial_{\rho},dx^{\sigma}\rangle=T_{\mu}^{\;\,\rho}T^{\nu}_{\;\;\;\sigma}\eta_{\rho}^{\;\;\sigma}=\eta_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}\equiv\langle\partial_{\mu},dx^{\nu}\rangle.

(259)

From the above transformation law,
\partial_{\mu}
is usually regarded as covariant vector and
dx^{\mu}
as contravariant vector in coordinate spacetime. In general, any covariant vector field
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu}(x)
with a lower index and contravariant vector field
\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\mu}(x)
with an upper index transform as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{\mu}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}\mspace{1mu}{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}(x),\quad\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu}(x^{\prime})=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\;\nu}\mspace{1mu}\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\nu}(x),

(260)

which keeps the scalar product between the covariant vector field and contravariant vector field to be invariant:

\displaystyle{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{\mu}(x^{\prime})\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}T^{\mu}_{\;\;\;\nu^{\prime}}{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}(x)\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\nu^{\prime}}(x)=\eta_{\;\nu^{\prime}}^{\nu}{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}(x)\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\nu^{\prime}}(x)\equiv{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu}(x)\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\mu}(x).

(261)

The gravigauge field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)
(
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)
) and its dual field
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)
) are considered as bi-covariant vector fields in biframe spacetime. Where
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)
(
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)
) is regarded as a covariant vector field in coordinate spacetime and a contravariant vector field in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, while
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)
) is viewed as a contravariant vector field in coordinate spacetime and a covariant vector field in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. Namely, they transform as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}\;a}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{A}_{\nu}^{\;a}(x),\quad\chi_{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}\;a}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}\mspace{1mu}\chi_{\nu}^{\;a}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x^{\prime})=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\;\nu}\mspace{1mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\nu}(x),\quad\hat{\chi}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x^{\prime})=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\;\nu}\mspace{1mu}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\nu}(x),

(262)

under the general coordinate transformation of general linear group symmetry GL(1,3; R), and

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}\;a}(x)=\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;b}(x)\mspace{1mu}\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;b}(x),\quad\chi_{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}\;a}(x)=\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;b}(x)\mspace{1mu}\chi_{\mu}^{\;b}(x)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x)=\Lambda_{a}^{\;\;b}(x)\mspace{1mu}\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{b}^{\;\mu}(x),\quad\hat{\chi}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x)=\Lambda_{a}^{\;\;b}(x)\mspace{1mu}\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\mu}(x),

(263)

under the gauge transformation of spin gauge group symmetry SP(1,3). So the gravicoordinate displacement
\delta\varkappa^{a}
(
\delta\chi^{a}
) and derivative
\hat{\eth}_{a}
(
\eth_{a}
) defined in locally flat gravigauge spacetime become invariant under the general coordinate transformation. Explicitly, we have,

\displaystyle\delta\varkappa^{a}\equiv\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)dx^{\mu}=\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}\;a}(x^{\prime})dx^{{}^{\prime}\mu},\quad\delta\chi^{a}\equiv\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)dx^{\mu}=\chi_{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}\;a}(x^{\prime})dx^{{}^{\prime}\mu},

\displaystyle\hat{\eth}_{a}\equiv\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)\partial_{\mu}=\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x^{\prime})\partial^{\prime}_{\mu},\quad\eth_{a}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)\partial_{\mu}=\hat{\chi}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x^{\prime})\partial^{\prime}_{\mu},

(264)

which indicates that the gravicoordinate displacement
\delta\varkappa^{a}
(
\delta\chi^{a}
) and derivative
\hat{\eth}_{a}
(
\eth_{a}
) introduced in Eqs.(
44
) and (
IV
) are the scalar product between the covariant vector and contravariant vector in curved Riemannian spacetime of coordinates. Therefore, the gravicoordinate displacement
\delta\varkappa^{a}
(
\delta\chi^{a}
) and derivative
\hat{\eth}_{a}
(
\eth_{a}
) naturally possess the general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R). It can be checked that the inner product of
\delta\varkappa^{a}
(
\delta\chi^{a}
) and derivative
\hat{\eth}_{a}
(
\eth_{a}
) becomes invariant under the spin gauge transformation,

\displaystyle\langle\hat{\eth}^{\prime}_{a},\delta\varkappa^{{}^{\prime}b}\rangle=\Lambda_{a}^{\;\,c}\Lambda^{b}_{\;\;\;d}\mspace{1mu}\langle\hat{\eth}_{c},\delta\varkappa^{d}\rangle=\Lambda_{a}^{\;\,c}\Lambda^{b}_{\;\;\;d}\mspace{1mu}\eta_{c}^{\;\;d}=\eta_{a}^{\;\;b}\mspace{1mu}\equiv\langle\hat{\eth}_{a},\delta\varkappa^{b}\rangle.

(265)

On the other hand, the gravimetric field
\mathsf{H}_{\mu\nu}(x)
(
\chi_{\mu\nu}(x)
) and dual gravimetric field
\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\mu\nu}(x)
(
\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}(x)
) defined in Eqs.(
III.2
) and (
IV
) as the scalar products of bi-covariant vector field
\mathbf{A}_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)
(
\chi_{\mu}^{\;a}(x)
) and
\hat{\mathbf{A}}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)
(
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)
) in locally flat gravigauge spacetime correspond to the covariant tensor and contravariant tensor fields in curved Riemannian spacetime of coordinates. They obey the following transformation laws:

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{\sigma}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\sigma}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\nu}}\mspace{1mu}\mathsf{H}_{\rho\sigma}(x)=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\rho}T_{\nu}^{\;\;\sigma}\,\mathsf{H}_{\rho\sigma}(x),\quad\chi^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\rho}T_{\nu}^{\;\;\sigma}\,\chi_{\rho\sigma}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=\frac{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}}{\partial x^{\rho}}\frac{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\nu}}{\partial x^{\sigma}}\mspace{1mu}\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\rho\sigma}(x)=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\rho}T^{\nu}_{\;\;\sigma}\,\hat{\mathsf{H}}^{\rho\sigma}(x),\quad\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\rho}T^{\nu}_{\;\;\sigma}\,\hat{\chi}^{\rho\sigma}(x).

(266)

So the Christoffel symbols given in Eq.(
) transform as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{{}^{\prime}\rho}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\frac{\partial x^{\mu^{\prime}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}}\frac{\partial x^{\nu^{\prime}}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\nu}})\left(\,\frac{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\rho}}{\partial x^{\rho^{\prime}}}\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}(x)-\frac{\partial^{2}x^{{}^{\prime}\rho}}{\partial x^{\mu^{\prime}}\partial x^{\nu^{\prime}}}\,\right)

(267)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle T_{\mu}^{\;\;\mu^{\prime}}T_{\nu}^{\;\;\nu^{\prime}}T^{\rho}_{\;\;\;\rho^{\prime}}\left(\,\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu^{\prime}\nu^{\prime}}^{\rho^{\prime}}(x)-T^{\rho^{\prime}}_{\;\;\,\sigma}\partial_{\mu^{\prime}}T^{\sigma}_{\;\;\,\nu^{\prime}}\right),

which provides the local transformation property of Christoffel symbols with appearance of inhomogeneous term under general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R), which is analogous to the gauge-type transformation of spin gauge group symmetry SP(1,3) in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. Such a property of transformations keeps the covariant derivative on the covariant vector field
{\mathsf{V}}_{\mu}(x)
and contravariant vector field
\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\mu}(x)
to be general covariance under the general coordinate transformations, i.e.:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{\mu}{\mathsf{V}}^{\prime}_{\nu}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\rho}T_{\nu}^{\;\;\sigma}\nabla_{\rho}{\mathsf{V}}_{\sigma}(x),\quad\nabla_{\mu}{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}(x)=\partial_{\mu}{\mathsf{V}}_{\nu}(x)-\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\nu}^{\rho}(x){\mathsf{V}}_{\rho}(x),

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}_{\mu}\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{{}^{\prime}\nu}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\rho}T^{\nu}_{\;\;\sigma}\nabla_{\rho}\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\sigma}(x),\quad\nabla_{\mu}\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\nu}(x)=\partial_{\mu}\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\nu}(x)+\mathbf{\Gamma}_{\mu\rho}^{\nu}(x)\hat{{\mathsf{V}}}^{\rho}(x).

(268)

With the above transformation laws, it can be verified explicitly that all actions constructed in the previous sections are invariant under the general coordinate transformation via an arbitrary reparametrization of coordinates shown in Eqs.(
257
)-(
259
), which displays the emergent general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R) of the actions. As a consequence, we come to the statement that the gauge invariance principle naturally leads the action formulated in locally flat gravigauge spacetime to possess a maximal joint symmetry:

\displaystyle G_{S}=\mbox{GL(}1,3,\mbox{R)}\Join\mbox{WS(}1,3),

which implies that the Poincaré group symmetry PO(1,3) in globally flat Minkowski spacetime will automatically be extended to the local general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R), which brings on the appearance of Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian spacetime.

Such a joint symmetry of the action indicates that the dynamic equations of motion for the basic fields derived based on the least action principle all become covariance in biframe spacetime. Namely, the gravidynamics and spinodynamics as well as electrodynamics discussed in the previous sections should obey the general coordinate covariance in curved Riemannian spacetime and spin gauge covariance in locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

XI.2
Gravigeometry-medium electrodynamics in any motional reference frame

Let us examine explicitly the general coordinate covariance and spin gauge covariance for the electrodynamics. The general coordinate covariance indicates that the dynamic equations of electromagnetic gauge field presented in Eqs. (
171
)-(
X.1
