) hold in any reference frame in the Einstein basis for the scaling gauge symmetry, i.e.:

\displaystyle\partial^{\prime}_{\nu}\widehat{F}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=-\widehat{J}^{{}^{\prime}\mu}(x^{\prime}),

(269)

with

\displaystyle\widehat{F}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})\equiv\chi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\tilde{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}[\mu\nu]\rho\sigma}(x^{\prime})F^{\prime}_{\rho\sigma}(x^{\prime}),

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\rho\sigma}(x^{\prime})\equiv\partial^{\prime}_{\rho}A^{\prime}_{\sigma}(x^{\prime})-\partial^{\prime}_{\sigma}A^{\prime}_{\rho}(x^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}[\mu\nu]\rho\sigma}(x^{\prime})\equiv\frac{1}{2}[\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\rho}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}\nu\sigma}(x^{\prime})-\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}\nu\rho}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\sigma}(x^{\prime})],

\displaystyle\widehat{J}^{{}^{\prime}\mu}(x^{\prime})=\chi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x^{\prime})J^{a}(x^{\prime}),\quad J^{a}(x^{\prime})\equiv-g_{E}\bar{\psi}(x^{\prime})\gamma^{a}\psi(x^{\prime}),

(270)

where the relevant fields and vectors in arbitrary new reference frame obey the following transformation properties:

\displaystyle\partial_{\mu}\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\mu}}\to\partial^{\prime}_{\mu}\equiv\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}}=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}\partial_{\nu},

\displaystyle A_{\mu}(x)\to A^{\prime}_{\mu}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}A_{\nu}(x),

\displaystyle F_{\mu\nu}(x)\to F^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=T_{\mu}^{\;\;\rho}T_{\nu}^{\;\;\sigma}F_{\rho\sigma}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}(x)\to\hat{\chi}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\mu}(x^{\prime})=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\nu}\mspace{1mu}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\nu}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}(x)\equiv\eta^{ab}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}(x)\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\nu}(x)\to\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=T^{\mu}_{\;\;\rho}T^{\nu}_{\;\;\sigma}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\sigma}(x).

(271)

It is noticed that the above dynamic equations possess a hidden gauge invariance.

To demonstrate the general coordinate covariance, it is useful to rewrite the equation of motion in terms of the covariant derivative. Explicitly, we have the following identity:

\displaystyle\partial_{\nu}\widehat{F}^{\mu\nu}(x)\equiv\chi(x)\nabla_{\nu}\hat{F}^{\mu\nu}(x)\equiv\chi(x)[\partial_{\nu}\hat{F}^{\mu\nu}(x)+\Gamma_{\nu\rho}^{\mu}\hat{F}^{\rho\nu}(x)+\Gamma_{\nu\rho}^{\nu}\hat{F}^{\mu\rho}(x)],

(272)

where we have used the following definition and property:

\displaystyle\hat{F}^{\mu\nu}(x)\equiv\hat{\chi}^{\mu\rho}(x)\hat{\chi}^{\nu\sigma}(x)F_{\rho\sigma}(x)\equiv\tilde{\chi}^{[\mu\nu]\rho\sigma}(x)F_{\rho\sigma}(x)=-\hat{F}^{\nu\mu}(x),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\nu\rho}^{\mu}\equiv\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\mu},\quad\Gamma_{\nu\rho}^{\nu}\equiv\partial_{\rho}\ln\chi(x),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\nu\rho}^{\mu}\widehat{F}^{\rho\nu}(x)\equiv\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{\nu\rho}^{\mu}-\Gamma_{\rho\nu}^{\mu})\widehat{F}^{\rho\nu}(x)=0,

\displaystyle\chi(x)\Gamma_{\nu\rho}^{\nu}\widehat{F}^{\mu\rho}(x)\equiv(\partial_{\rho}\chi(x))\widehat{F}^{\mu\rho}(x).

(273)

So the equations of motion in terms of the explicit covariant form can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\nu}\hat{F}^{\mu\nu}(x)=-\hat{J}^{\mu}(x),

(274)

with the current density,

\displaystyle\hat{J}^{\mu}(x)\equiv\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)J^{a}(x)=-g_{E}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\mu}(x)\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma^{a}\psi(x).

(275)

Therefore, the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations hold in any motional reference frame and can be expressed into the following forms:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\cdot\widehat{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}=\widehat{J}^{{}^{\prime}0},

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\times\widehat{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}=\widehat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\prime}+\frac{\partial\widehat{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}0}},

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\prime}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=-\frac{\partial\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}0}},

(276)

with

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\widehat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{\prime}+\widehat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\prime},\quad\mbox{or}\quad\widehat{E}^{{}^{\prime}i}(x^{\prime})=\hat{\varepsilon}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}E^{{}^{\prime}j}+\hat{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;\;j}B^{{}^{\prime}j},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\prime}-\widehat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{{}^{\prime}T}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{\prime},\quad\mbox{or}\quad\widehat{B}^{{}^{\prime}i}(x^{\prime})=\hat{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}B^{{}^{\prime}j}-\hat{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\;i}_{j}E^{{}^{\prime}j},

\displaystyle\hat{J}^{{}^{\prime}0}(x^{\prime})\equiv\breve{\kappa}^{\prime}J^{{}^{\prime}0}+\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{\prime}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime},\quad\hat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}+\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{\prime}J^{{}^{\prime}0},

(277)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\{\hat{\varepsilon}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})\},\quad\mbox{or}\quad\hat{\varepsilon}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})\equiv\chi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})(\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}0}_{\;0}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})-\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;0}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}0}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})),

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\mu}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\{\hat{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})\},\quad\mbox{or}\quad\hat{\mu}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})\equiv-\frac{1}{2}\chi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\epsilon^{i}_{\;\;kl}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{kk^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}ll^{\prime}}(x^{\prime})\epsilon_{k^{\prime}l^{\prime}j},

\displaystyle\widehat{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\{\hat{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})\},\quad\mbox{or}\quad\hat{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}i}_{\;j}(x^{\prime})\equiv\chi^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}ik}(x^{\prime})\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}l0}(x^{\prime})\epsilon_{klj},

\displaystyle\breve{\kappa}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\hat{\chi}_{0}^{{}^{\prime}\;0}(x^{\prime}),\quad\breve{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\;0}(x^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle\mathring{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\;i}(x^{\prime})\},\quad\hat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\{\hat{\chi}_{0}^{{}^{\prime}\;i}(x^{\prime})\},

(278)

where the coefficient functions are in general governed by the gravidynamics in the new reference frame.

XI.3
Gravigauge-mediated electrodynamics in any spinning reference frame

We now turn to discuss the electrodynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. The dynamic equation presented in Eq.(
221
) is spin gauge covariance, which holds in any spinning reference frame in locally flat garvigauge spacetime, i.e.:

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{b}F^{{}^{\prime}ab}(x)=-J^{{}^{\prime}a}(x),

(279)

with

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{b}F^{{}^{\prime}ab}(x)\equiv\eth^{\prime}_{b}F^{{}^{\prime}ab}(x)+\mathsf{\Omega}_{bc}^{{}^{\prime}a}(x)F^{{}^{\prime}cb}(x)+\mathsf{\Omega}_{bc}^{{}^{\prime}b}(x)F^{{}^{\prime}ac}(x),

\displaystyle F^{{}^{\prime}ab}(x)\equiv\eta^{ac}\eta^{bd}F^{\prime}_{cd}(x)\equiv\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}a\mu}(x)\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}b\nu}(x)F_{\mu\nu}(x),

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{cd}(x)\equiv D^{\prime}_{c}A^{\prime}_{d}(x)-D^{\prime}_{d}A^{\prime}_{c}(x)=\eth^{\prime}_{c}A^{\prime}_{d}(x)-\eth^{\prime}_{d}A^{\prime}_{c}(x)+\mathsf{\Omega}_{cd}^{{}^{\prime}b}(x)A^{\prime}_{b}(x)-\mathsf{\Omega}_{dc}^{{}^{\prime}b}(x)A^{\prime}_{b}(x),

\displaystyle J^{{}^{\prime}a}(x)=-g_{E}\bar{\psi}^{\prime}(x)\gamma^{a}\psi^{\prime}(x),

(280)

where all fields in arbitrary spinning reference frame obey the following transformation properties under the gauge transformation of spin gauge symmetry SP(1,3):

\displaystyle F^{ab}(x)\to F^{{}^{\prime}ab}(x)=\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;c}(x)\Lambda^{b}_{\;\;d}(x)F^{cd}(x),\quad A_{c}(x)\to A^{\prime}_{c}(x)=\Lambda_{c}^{\;\;d}(x)A_{d}(x),

\displaystyle J^{a}(x)\to J^{{}^{\prime}a}(x)=\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;c}(x)J^{c}(x),\quad\psi(x)\to\psi^{\prime}(x)=S(\Lambda)\psi(x),\quad S(\Lambda)=e^{i\varpi_{ab}(x)\Sigma^{ab}},

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Omega}_{cb}^{a}(x)\to\mathsf{\Omega}_{cb}^{{}^{\prime}a}(x)=\Lambda_{c}^{\;\;c^{\prime}}(x)[\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;a^{\prime}}(x)\Lambda_{b}^{\;\;b^{\prime}}(x)\mathsf{\Omega}_{c^{\prime}b^{\prime}}^{a^{\prime}}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;d}(x)\eth_{c^{\prime}}\Lambda_{b}^{\;\;d}(x)-\Lambda_{b}^{\;\;d}(x)\eth_{c^{\prime}}\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;d}(x)],

\displaystyle\eth_{c}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}(x)\partial_{\mu}\to\eth^{\prime}_{c}=\Lambda_{c}^{\;\;d}(x)\eth_{d},\quad\hat{\chi}_{c}^{\;\;\mu}(x)\to\hat{\chi}_{c}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\mu}(x)=\Lambda_{c}^{\;\;d}(x)\hat{\chi}_{d}^{\;\;\mu}(x).

(281)

So the gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations in Eq.(
X.2
) are valid in any spinning reference frame, which can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}=J^{{}^{\prime}0}+\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}={\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}+\eth^{\prime}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{A}^{\prime}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}=-\eth^{\prime}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-2\breve{A}^{\prime}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+2\breve{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+2\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}=\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime},

(282)

with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\equiv\{\widetilde{E}^{{}^{\prime}\alpha}=-F^{{}^{\prime}0\alpha}\},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\equiv\{\widetilde{B}^{{}^{\prime}\alpha}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\alpha}_{\;\;\beta\gamma}F^{{}^{\prime}\beta\gamma}\},

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\equiv\{\breve{E}^{\prime}_{\alpha}=-F_{0\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}0}\},\quad\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\equiv\{\breve{B}^{\prime}_{\alpha}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\alpha}^{\;\;\gamma\delta}F_{\gamma\delta}^{{}^{\prime}0}\},

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}\equiv\{\breve{E}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\beta}=-F_{0\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\beta}\},\quad\breve{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}\equiv\{\breve{B}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\beta}=-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon_{\alpha}^{\;\;\gamma\delta}F_{\gamma\delta}^{{}^{\prime}\beta}\},

\displaystyle\breve{A}^{\prime}_{0}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta}\breve{E}_{\beta}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\alpha},\quad\breve{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\equiv\{\breve{A}^{\prime}_{\alpha}=-\breve{E}^{\prime}_{\alpha}-\epsilon_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\;\;\,\gamma}\breve{B}_{\gamma}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\beta}\},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\equiv\{\eth^{\prime}_{\alpha}\},\quad\eth^{\prime}_{0}\equiv\hat{\chi}_{0}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\mu}\partial_{\mu}.

(283)

XI.4
Gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations in special background medium and general coordinate covariance of equations in motional reference frame

In general, either the gravigeometry-medium electric field
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}
or gravigeometry-medium magnetic field
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
depends on both free-motion electric field
\mathbf{E}
and magnetic field
\mathbf{B}
defined in globally flat Minkowski spacetime. Only when the symmetric gravimetric field
\hat{\chi}^{0i}(x)=\hat{\chi}^{i0}(x)
goes to be vanishing and brings the coefficient function
\hat{\alpha}^{i}_{\;j}(x)
to be zero, so that the gravigeometry-medium electric field
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}
or gravigeometry-medium magnetic field
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
becomes to be proportional only to the electric field
\mathbf{E}
or magnetic field
\mathbf{B}
. Explicitly, such a case can be expressed into the following relations:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{0i}(x)=\hat{\chi}^{i0}(x)\to 0,\quad\hat{\alpha}^{i}_{\;j}(x)\to 0,

\displaystyle\widehat{E}^{i}=\hat{\varepsilon}^{i}_{\;j}(x)E^{j},\quad\widehat{B}^{i}=\hat{\mu}^{i}_{\;j}(x)B^{j}.

(284)

As a simple case, let us examine a special background medium in which the gravimetric field is supposed to have the following simple geometric distributions:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{00}=\alpha_{0}^{2}\eta^{00},\quad\hat{\chi}^{ij}=\alpha_{s}^{2}\eta^{ij},\quad\hat{\chi}^{0i}=0,

(285)

with
\alpha_{0}
and
\alpha_{s}
being constants. Such a special background medium is homogeneous and isotropic in three spatial dimensions. The global scaling invariance of the action enables us to make the following scale transformations for the gravimetric field and coordinates:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}\to\alpha_{s}\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu},\quad\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}\to\alpha_{s}^{2}\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu},

\displaystyle\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a}\to\alpha_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\mu}^{\;\;a},\quad\chi_{\mu\nu}\to\alpha_{s}^{-2}\chi_{\mu\nu},

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to\alpha_{s}^{-1}x^{\mu},

(286)

which simplifies the gravimetric distribution into the following form:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{00}=c_{s}^{-2}\eta^{00},\quad\hat{\chi}^{ij}=\eta^{ij},\quad\hat{\chi}^{0i}=0,

(287)

with

\displaystyle c_{s}\equiv\frac{\alpha_{s}}{\alpha_{0}}.

(288)

which indicates that the basic feature of such a special background medium is solely charactrized by the constant ratio
c_{s}
. So the corresponding gravigeometry-medium electric field
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}
and gravigeometry-medium magnetic field
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}
get the following simple relations:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{E}}=\varepsilon_{s}\mathbf{E}\equiv\mathbf{D},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{B}}=\frac{\mathbf{B}}{\mu_{s}}\equiv\mathbf{H},

(289)

with the constant coefficients:

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{s}\equiv c_{s}^{-1},\quad\mu_{s}\equiv c_{s}^{-1},\quad\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}}}=c_{s}.

(290)

The current density in such a special background medium has a simple form:

\displaystyle\widehat{J}^{0}=J^{0},\quad J^{0}=-g_{E}\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma^{0}\psi(x),

\displaystyle\widehat{J}^{i}=c_{s}J^{i},\quad J^{i}=-g_{E}\bar{\psi}(x)\gamma^{i}\psi(x),

(291)

with
J^{0}
and
J^{i}
being the free charge density and current density, respectively.
From the above analyses and the general gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations, we arrive at the following Maxwell equations in such a special background medium:

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{D}=J^{0},

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{H}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}}}{\mathbf{J}}+\frac{\partial\mathbf{D}}{\partial x^{0}},

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{B}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{E}=-\frac{\partial\mathbf{B}}{\partial x^{0}},

(292)

which can be expressed as the following forms:

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{E}=c_{s}J^{0},

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{B}={\mathbf{J}}+\frac{1}{c_{s}^{2}}\frac{\partial\mathbf{E}}{\partial x^{0}},

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\mathbf{B}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla\times\mathbf{E}=-\frac{\partial\mathbf{B}}{\partial x^{0}},

(293)

where the electromagnetic fields
\mathbf{E}
and
\mathbf{B}
as well as the gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic fields
\widehat{\mathbf{E}}\equiv\mathbf{D}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}\equiv\mathbf{H}
in the special background medium all have the same unit since the ratio
c_{s}
is a dimensionless constant in the present definition.

Let us now examine the Lorentz covariance of gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations under the global Lorentz transformation of group symmetry SO(1,3) in globally flat Minkowski spacetime, i.e.:

\displaystyle x^{\mu}\to x^{{}^{\prime}\mu}=L^{\mu}_{\;\;\nu}x^{\nu},\quad A_{\mu}(x)\to A^{\prime}_{\mu}(x^{\prime})=L_{\mu}^{\;\;\nu}A_{\nu}(x),

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{\mu\nu}(x)\to\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}\mu\nu}(x^{\prime})=L^{\mu}_{\;\;\rho}L^{\nu}_{\;\;\sigma}\hat{\chi}^{\rho\sigma}(x).

(294)

For simplicity, we should consider the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations in special background medium under the following global Lorentz boost transformation
L^{\mu}_{\;\;\nu}
:

\displaystyle L^{\mu}_{\;\;\nu}\equiv\eta^{\mu}_{\;\;\nu}+\tilde{L}^{\mu}_{\;\;\nu},

\displaystyle(\tilde{L})^{00}=\gamma-1,\quad(\tilde{L})^{i0}=-\gamma\beta^{i}=(\tilde{L})^{0i},

\displaystyle(\tilde{L})^{ij}=(\gamma-1)\frac{\beta^{i}\beta^{j}}{\beta^{2}}=(\tilde{L})^{ji},\quad\gamma\equiv\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-\beta^{2}}},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\beta}\equiv(\beta^{1},\beta^{2},\beta^{3}),\quad\beta^{2}\equiv\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot\boldsymbol{\beta}\equiv\beta_{1}^{2}+\beta_{2}^{2}+\beta_{3}^{2},

(295)

where
\boldsymbol{\beta}
is regarded as a boost vector.

To obtain the gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic field in the boosted new coordinate reference frame, it is useful to present the gravimetric of the special background medium in the boosted new coordinate reference frame as follows:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}00}=\gamma^{2}c_{s}^{-2}(1-\beta^{2}c_{s}^{2}),\quad\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}0i}=\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}i0}=-\gamma^{2}c_{s}^{-2}(1-c_{s}^{2})\beta^{i},

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}ij}=\hat{\chi}^{{}^{\prime}ji}=\eta^{ij}+\gamma^{2}c_{s}^{-2}(1-c_{s}^{2})\beta^{i}\beta^{j},

(296)

which enables us to figure out from Eqs.(
XI.2
) and (
XI.2
) the following gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic field in the boosted new coordinate reference frame:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}(1-\beta^{2}c_{s}^{2})c_{s}^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})-\gamma^{2}(c_{s}^{-2}-1)c_{s}\boldsymbol{\beta}\times\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}(1-c_{s}^{2})c_{s}^{-1}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[1-2\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}(c_{s}^{-2}-1)]c_{s}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})-\gamma^{2}c_{s}(c_{s}^{-2}-1)\boldsymbol{\beta}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

(297)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\gamma^{2}(c_{s}^{-2}-1)c_{s}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta},

which may be rewritten into the following forms:

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\widehat{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}(1-\frac{\beta^{2}}{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}})\varepsilon_{s}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})-\gamma^{2}(\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}-1)\frac{1}{\mu_{s}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\times\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\gamma^{2}(1-\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}})\varepsilon_{s}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta},

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\widehat{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[1-2\gamma^{2}\beta^{2}(\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}-1)]\frac{1}{\mu_{s}}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})-\gamma^{2}(1-\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}})\varepsilon_{s}\boldsymbol{\beta}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

(298)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\gamma^{2}(1-\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}})\frac{1}{\mu_{s}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta}.

Correspondingly, the current density in the boosted new coordinate reference frame is found to be,

\displaystyle\widehat{J}^{{}^{\prime}0}(x^{\prime})=\gamma[J^{0}(x^{\prime})-c_{s}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\;\;\;=\gamma[J^{0}(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})],

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})=c_{s}{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})-\gamma J^{0}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta}+(\gamma-1)c_{s}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\;\;\;=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}}}{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})-\gamma J^{0}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta}+(\gamma-1)\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta}.

(299)

For the case
\beta^{2}\ll 1
, when keeping only the leading term of
\beta_{i}
with
\gamma\simeq 1
, we arrive at the following simplified relations:

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\widehat{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{s}\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})-(\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}-1)\frac{1}{\mu_{s}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\times\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\equiv\widehat{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{s}}\mathbf{B}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})-(1-\frac{1}{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}})\varepsilon_{s}\boldsymbol{\beta}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}(x^{\prime}),

(300)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{J}^{{}^{\prime}0}(x^{\prime})\simeq J^{0}(x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\simeq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\varepsilon_{s}\mu_{s}}}{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})-J^{0}(x^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\beta},

(301)

where
J^{0}(x^{\prime})
and
{\mathbf{J}}(x^{\prime})
are free current density. It can be verified explicitly that the gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic field in the boosted new coordinate reference frame does satisfy the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations obeying the general coordinate covariance, i.e.:

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{D}^{\prime}=\widehat{J}^{{}^{\prime}0},

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\times\mathbf{H}^{\prime}=\widehat{{\mathbf{J}}}^{\prime}+\frac{\partial\mathbf{D}^{\prime}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}0}},

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\cdot\mathbf{B}^{\prime}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla^{\prime}\times\mathbf{E}^{\prime}=-\frac{\partial\mathbf{B}^{\prime}}{\partial x^{{}^{\prime}0}}.

(302)

In conclusion, we have demonstrated how the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations in the special background medium obey the general coordinate covariance by explicitly making the global Lorentz boost transformation into a new coordinate system with a constant boost vector
\boldsymbol{\beta}
. Actually, when the boost vector
\boldsymbol{\beta}
becomes local
\boldsymbol{\beta}=\boldsymbol{\beta}(x^{\prime})
, the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations in such a boosted new reference frame remain valid. This is because the above derivation does not depend on whether the Lorentz boost transformation is global or local. In general, the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations for the gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic field hold in any motional coordinate reference frame due to the general coordinate covariance of electrodynamics.

XI.5
Gravigauge-mediated electrodynamics in special background medium and spin gauge covariance of generalized Maxwell equations in any spinning reference frame

To show the spin effect arising from the interaction of spin gravigauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{\mu}^{ab}(x)
, it is useful to examine the gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations in the special background medium and demonstrate explicitly the spin gauge covariance under the spin boost transformation of spin gauge symmetry SP(1,3) in locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

From the simple gravimetric form of special background medium shown in Eq.(
287
), the corresponding dual gravigauge field
\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}
is simply given by,

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;0}=c_{s}^{-1}\eta_{0}^{\;0},\quad\hat{\chi}_{0}^{\;\;i}=0=\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;0},\quad\hat{\chi}_{\alpha}^{\;\;i}=\eta_{\alpha}^{\;i},

(303)

with
a=(0,\alpha)
and
\mu=(0,i)
(
\alpha,i=1,2,3
). In this simple case, the background gravigauge field strength vanishes, and the gravigauge-dressed electromagnetic field gets the following simple forms:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}=c_{s}^{-1}\mathbf{E},\quad\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}=\mathbf{B}.

(304)

The gravigauge Maxwell equations presented in Eq.(
X.2
) are simplified to be:

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}=J^{0},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}={\mathbf{J}}+\eth_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}=-\eth_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}},

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}=0,

(305)

where the gravicoordinate derivatives in such a special background medium are given by,

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}=\nabla,\quad\eth_{0}=c_{s}^{-1}\partial_{0}\equiv c_{s}^{-1}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}.

(306)

Let us now consider the following spin gauge transformation in locally flat gravigauge spacetime:

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{a}^{\;\;\mu}(x)\to\hat{\chi}_{a}^{{}^{\prime}\;\,\mu}(x)=\Lambda_{a}^{\;\;b}(x)\hat{\chi}_{b}^{\;\;\mu}(x),\quad\eth_{a}\to\eth^{\prime}_{a}=\Lambda_{a}^{\;\;b}(x)\eth_{b},

\displaystyle F^{ab}(x)\to F^{{}^{\prime}ab}(x)=\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;c}(x)\Lambda^{b}_{\;\;d}F^{cd}(x),

\displaystyle J^{a}(x)\to J^{{}^{\prime}a}(x)=\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;c}(x)J^{c}(x),

(307)

with the spin boost transformation given explicitly as follows:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{a}^{\;\;b}(x)\equiv\eta_{a}^{\;\;b}+\tilde{\Lambda}_{a}^{\;\;b}(x),

\displaystyle(\tilde{\Lambda})_{00}=\cosh\varpi-1,\quad(\tilde{\Lambda})_{\alpha 0}=-\varsigma_{\alpha}\sinh\varpi=(\tilde{\Lambda})_{0\alpha},

\displaystyle(\tilde{\Lambda})_{\alpha\beta}=(\cosh\varpi-1)\varsigma_{\alpha}\varsigma_{\beta}=(\tilde{\Lambda})_{\beta\alpha},

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\equiv(\varsigma_{1},\varsigma_{2},\varsigma_{3}),\quad\varsigma^{2}\equiv\varsigma_{1}^{2}+\varsigma_{2}^{2}+\varsigma_{3}^{2}=1,

\displaystyle\varpi\equiv\varpi(x),\quad\varsigma_{\alpha}\equiv\varsigma_{\alpha}(x),\quad\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\equiv\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(x).

(308)

The gravigauge-dressed electromagnetic field in the spin boosted new reference frame has the following relations:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}(x)=\cosh\varpi\,\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}+\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}-(\cosh\varpi-1)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(x)=(1-2(\cosh\varpi-1))\mspace{1mu}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}-\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}-2(\cosh\varpi-1)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}.

(309)

The corresponding relations for the current density and gravicoordinate derivative are found to be:

\displaystyle J^{{}^{\prime}0}(x)=\cosh\varpi\,J^{0}-\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot{\mathbf{J}},

\displaystyle{\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}(x)={\mathbf{J}}+[-J^{0}\sinh\varpi+(\cosh\varpi-1)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot{\mathbf{J}}]\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma},

(310)

and

\displaystyle\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}=\breve{\nabla}-\boldsymbol{\varsigma}[\sinh\varpi\,\eth_{0}-(\cosh\varpi-1)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\breve{\nabla}]=\nabla-\boldsymbol{\varsigma}c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi\,(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\mspace{1mu}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla),

\displaystyle\eth^{\prime}_{0}=\cosh\varpi\,\eth_{0}-\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\breve{\nabla}=c_{s}^{-1}\cosh\varpi\,(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla).

(311)

It can be verified that in the spin boosted new reference frame, the gravigauge field stength in the special background medium becomes no vanishing and gets the following form:

\displaystyle F_{cb}^{{}^{\prime}a}(x)=-\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;d}(\eth^{\prime}_{c}\Lambda_{b}^{\;\;d}-\eth^{\prime}_{b}\Lambda_{c}^{\;\;d})\equiv-\mathsf{\Omega}_{[cb]}^{{}^{\prime}a}(x),

(312)

which results from a pure spin gauge field
\mathsf{\Omega}_{c}^{ab}(x)
after the spin gauge transformation
\Lambda^{a}_{\;\;b}(x)
. To be more explicit, the electromagnetic-like gravigauge fields are given as follows:

\displaystyle\breve{E}^{\prime}_{\alpha}=-[\eth^{\prime}_{0}\varpi+\sinh\varpi\,(\eth^{\prime}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}]\mspace{1mu}\varsigma_{\alpha}-\sinh\varpi[\eth^{\prime}_{0}\varsigma_{\alpha}+\sinh\varpi\mspace{1mu}(\eth_{\alpha}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}],

\displaystyle\breve{B}^{\prime}_{\alpha}=-\epsilon_{\alpha}^{\;\;\beta\gamma}[\sinh\varpi\,\eth^{\prime}_{\beta}\varsigma_{\gamma}+\eth^{\prime}_{\beta}\varpi\varsigma_{\gamma}-\sinh\varpi\mspace{1mu}(\cosh\varpi-1)\varsigma_{\beta}(\eth^{\prime}_{\gamma}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}],

\displaystyle\breve{E}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\beta}=-[\eth^{\prime}_{\alpha}\varpi-2\varsigma_{\alpha}\sinh\varpi\,(\eth^{\prime}_{0}\varpi)]\mspace{1mu}\varsigma^{\beta}-\sinh\varpi\,\eth^{\prime}_{\alpha}\varsigma^{\beta}-(\cosh\varpi-1)[\varsigma_{\alpha}\eth_{0}^{\prime}\varsigma^{\beta}-(\eth_{0}^{\prime}\varsigma_{\alpha})\varsigma^{\beta}],

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+(\cosh\varpi-1)[(\cosh\varpi-1)\varsigma_{\alpha}(\eth_{0}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma})+\sinh\varpi\,(\eth_{\alpha}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}]\varsigma^{\beta},

\displaystyle\breve{B}_{\alpha}^{{}^{\prime}\;\beta}=\epsilon_{\alpha}^{\;\;\gamma\delta}[2\sinh\varpi\,\eth^{\prime}_{\gamma}\varsigma_{\delta}+3(\cosh\varpi-1)\eth^{\prime}_{\gamma}\varsigma_{\delta}-(\cosh\varpi-1)^{2}\varsigma_{\gamma}(\eth^{\prime}_{\delta}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}]\varsigma^{\beta},

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-(\cosh\varpi-1)\epsilon_{\alpha}^{\;\;\gamma\delta}\varsigma_{\gamma}\eth^{\prime}_{\delta}\varsigma^{\beta},

(313)

which can be represented by the following vector representations:

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}(x)=-[\eth^{\prime}_{0}\varpi+\sinh\varpi\,(\eth^{\prime}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}]\mspace{1mu}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-\sinh\varpi[\eth^{\prime}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}+\sinh\varpi\mspace{1mu}(\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}],

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}(x)=\sinh\varpi\,\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\times\boldsymbol{\varsigma}+\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\varpi\times\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-\sinh\varpi\mspace{1mu}(\cosh\varpi-1)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times\left((\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\right),

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}(x)=-[\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\varpi-2\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\sinh\varpi\,(\eth^{\prime}_{0}\varpi)]\mspace{1mu}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-\sinh\varpi\,\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-(\cosh\varpi-1)[\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\eth_{0}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-(\eth_{0}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\boldsymbol{\varsigma}],

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+(\cosh\varpi-1)[(\cosh\varpi-1)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}(\eth_{0}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma})+\sinh\varpi\,(\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}]\boldsymbol{\varsigma},

\displaystyle\breve{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}(x)=-[2\sinh\varpi\,\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\times\boldsymbol{\varsigma}+3(\cosh\varpi-1)\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\times\boldsymbol{\varsigma}-(\cosh\varpi-1)^{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times(\breve{\nabla}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\varsigma})\cdot\boldsymbol{\varsigma}]\boldsymbol{\varsigma},

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+(\cosh\varpi-1)(\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times\breve{\nabla}^{\prime})\boldsymbol{\varsigma}.

(314)

The gauge covariance of gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations in the special background medium can be expressed into the following forms in the spin boosted new reference frame:

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}=J^{{}^{\prime}0}+\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\nabla\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad={\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}+c_{s}^{-1}\cosh\varpi\,(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{A}^{\prime}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+2\breve{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+2\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}=\breve{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\nabla\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-\breve{\mathbb{B}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad=-c_{s}^{-1}\cosh\varpi\,(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-2\breve{A}^{\prime}_{0}\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}+\breve{\mathbb{E}}^{\prime}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime},

(315)

where both the gravigauge-dressed electromagnetic fields and electromagnetic-like gravigauge fields in the gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations are located at the same point
x^{\mu}
in the coordinate spacetime as the spin gauge transformation operates on the locally flat gravigauge spacetime and Dirac spinor field in Hilbert space.

For a special case that the spin transformation is taken to be a global transformation, namely the boost parameters
\varpi
and
\varsigma_{\alpha}
are all constant parameters, the electromagnetic-like gravigauge fields get to be vanishing. As a consequence, the above gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations in the globally spin boosted new reference frame are simplified to be:

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}=J^{{}^{\prime}0},

\displaystyle\nabla\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad={\mathbf{J}}^{\prime}+c_{s}^{-1}\cosh\varpi\,(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\nabla\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}=0,

\displaystyle\nabla\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}-c_{s}^{-1}\sinh\varpi(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\frac{\varpi}{2}\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\times\widetilde{\mathbf{E}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad=-c_{s}^{-1}\cosh\varpi\,(\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{0}}-c_{s}\tanh\varpi\,\boldsymbol{\varsigma}\cdot\nabla)\widetilde{\mathbf{B}}^{\prime}.

(316)

Therefore, we come to the observation that the gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations for gravigauge-dressed electromagnetic fields in locally flat gravigauge spacetime maintain the spin gauge covariance with hidden general coordinate invariance, and the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations for gravigeometry-medium electromagnetic fields in coordinate spacetime keep the general covariance of coordinates with hidden spin gauge invariance.

XII
Conclusions and discussions

In this paper, we have begun from the fact that the electron and charged leptons and quarks as basic constituents of matter in SM are actually Weyl fermions although they appear as Dirac fermions in electromagnetic interaction. This is because their weak interactions in SM produce the maximal parity violation. Based on such an observation, we have represented such a Dirac fermion in four-dimensional Hilbert space as the superposition of left-handed and right-handed Weyl fermions and treated it as a chirality-based Dirac spinor in the chiral spinor representation of eight-dimensional Hilbert space. We have demonstrated that such a chirality-based Dirac spinor possesses the inhomogeneous spin symmetry WS(1,3) in eight-dimensional Hilbert space instead of the usual spin symmetry SP(1,3) in four-dimensional Hilbert space. Such an inhomogeneous spin symmetry group WS(1,3) is a semi-direct product group WS(1,3) = SP(1,3)
\rtimes
W
1,3
with SP(1,3) being the spin group and W
1,3
referred to as
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin group, which realizes an internal Poincaré-type group (or internal inhomogeneous Lorentz-type group) in eight-dimensional Hilbert space. It has been shown that the
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin symmetry group emerges as a translation-like chiral-type spin symmetry group and the electric charge symmetry group U(1) appears as an internal Abelian group symmetry for the chirality-based Dirac spinor formulated in eight dimensional Hilbert space.

Following along the gauge invariance principle which states that the laws of nature should be independent of the choice of local field configurations, we have gauged the inhomogeneous spin symmetry WS(1,3) and electric charge symmetry U(1) for the chirality-based Dirac spinor in eight dimensional Hilbert space to be local symmetries. Meanwhile, the inhomogeneous Lorentz group symmetry (or Poincaré group symmetry) PO(1,3) remains to be global symmetry in four-dimensional Minkowski spacetime of coordinates. The inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry WS(1,3) as well as electric charge gauge symmetry U(1) lead to the introduction of spin gauge field and
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin gauge field as well as electromagnetic gauge field. It has been demonstrated that the translation-like
{\mathcal{W}}_{e}
-spin invariant-gauge field provides a gravigauge field as basic gravitational field, which reveals the nature of gravity and brings on the gravitational origin of spin gauge symmetry. So such an inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry WS(1,3) naturally leads to an internal Poincaré-type gauge symmetry. Furthermore, based on the displacement correspondence between the gravivector field in Hilbert space and coordinate vector in Minkowaski spacetime through the gravigauge field, a locally flat gravigauge spacetime spanned by the gravigauge basis emerges to form a fiber bundle structure of biframe spacetime, where the globally flat Minkowski spacetime appears as base spacetime and the locally flat gravigauge spacetime behaves as a fiber. By noticing the intriguing property that the gravigauge field plays a role as a Goldstone-like boson, we are able to define all gauge fields and field strengths in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, where the gravitational effects are shown to be characterized by the emergent non-commutative geometry of locally flat gravigauge spacetime.

Based on the action principle of path integral formulation, we have presented various formalisms of gauge and scaling invariant actions for the chirality-based Dirac spinor in eight dimensional Hilbert space, which has been shown to possess the maximal joint symmetry PO(1,3)
\Join
WS(1,3)
\times
U(1) and generate a hidden general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R). It has been verified that when the basic symmetry of chirality-based Dirac spinor in eight dimensional Hilbert space is gauged to be local symmetry via the gauge invariance principle, the symmetry of coordinate system automatically turns out to possess a hidden general linear group symmetry GL(1,3,R), which indicates that the laws of nature should be independent of the choice of coordinate systems and meanwhile brings on the genesis of Riemann geometry in curved Riemannian spacetime. Such a feature enables us to demonstrate the gauge-gravity and gravity-geometry correspondences and obtain the gauge-geometry duality relation within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory.

From the least action principle, we have derived the generalized Dirac equation to characterize the gravitational relativistic quantum theory. In particular, the quadratic gravigauge Dirac equation is derived in locally flat gravigauge spacetime to show the gravitational effect. The gauge-type gravitational equations of gravigauge field have been obtained to describe the gravidynamics in biframe spacetime and also in locally flat gravigauge spacetime. Meanwhile, we have investigated in detail the geometric gravidynamics based on the inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry and discussed its new effects beyond the Einstein theory of general relativity. The equation of motion for the spin gauge field has also been derived to describe the spinodynamics.

In particular, we have made a detailed analysis on the various formalisms of electrodynamics via deriving different generalized Maxwell equations in the presence of gravitational interactions, such as: the gravigeometry-medium electrodynamics by redarding the gravimetric field as the medium, the gravigauge-mediated electrodynamics in locally flat gravigauge spacetime and the gravimetric-gauge-mediated electrodynamics in dynamic Riemannian spacetime. In analyzing the gravimetric-gauge-mediated Maxwell equations, we have shown that it is useful to introduce the electric-like and magnetic-like gravimetric-gauge fields. In general, we have explicitly demonstrated that the dynamics of all basic fields should maintain the general coordinate covariance in curved Riemannian spacetime and the spin gauge covariance in locally flat gravigauge spacetime, so the gravigeometry-medium Maxwell equations hold in any motional reference frame and the gravigauge-mediated Maxwell equations validate in any spinning reference frame. We have also presented a full discussion on the generalized Maxwell equations in special background medium and verified explicitly the general coordinate covariance in motional reference frame and the spin gauge covariance in spinning reference frame.

It is straightforward to apply the present analyses to extend the standard model by including the gravitational interaction, which enables us to provide a unified description on four basic forces, i.e., electromagnetic, weak, strong and gravitational interactions, within the framework of gravitational quantum field theory. Meanwhile, such an extended standard model with inhomogeneous spin gauge symmetry is expected to result from a more fundamental theory, such as the hyperunified field theory
